It reproduces the lane's predicate exactly — `Schema::hasColumn('roles', 'provisioning_source')` guard, tenant-team filter, `guard_name = 'sanctum'`, `whereRaw('provisioning_source = ?')` (`lane/…/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:73-76`) — minus the hard-coded role name. The lane's private method is left exactly as it is.

> **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 4). Sync writes `provisioning_source` in exactly one case: when it CREATES a missing `general_manager` on a tenant that carries no marker.** This replaces rev 3's "sync never writes `provisioning_source`" clause, which was incompatible with sync owning role creation at all.
>
> - **When it happens.** Step 5 finds no `general_manager` row and `roleMarkers->tenantCarriesMarker('w-lot-a-1a')` is `false`. Sync creates the row with `name = 'general_manager'`, `guard_name = 'sanctum'`, `tenant_id = :tenantId`, `is_system = true`, `template_key = 'general_manager'`, `template_version = <registry version>`, `customised_at = NULL` **and `provisioning_source = 'w-lot-a-1a'`**.
> - **Why it is legal.** The lane's CHECK admits a non-null `provisioning_source` only for `tenant_id IS NOT NULL AND provisioning_source = 'w-lot-a-1a' AND name = 'general_manager' AND guard_name = 'sanctum'` (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2026_09_06_205000_add_provisioning_source_to_roles.php:34`, installed as a PG constraint at `:42` and as the two SQLite triggers at `:53`). Every clause holds by construction on this path — sync always sets a tenant id and always this name and guard — so the write cannot violate it. The partial unique `roles_provisioning_source_team_unique` (`:64-75`) is satisfied because sync reaches this branch only when the tenant has no marked role.
> - **What it means, stated precisely (rev 5, gate r4 m-1).** The marker is a *declaration that the delta's end state is embodied for this tenant*, and the registry's `general_manager` template **is** that end state (rule 1). Writing it makes the retained **seeder preservation branch** (`lane/…/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:51-54`) a no-op for that tenant, which is the correct outcome, not a side effect: it exists to avoid re-provisioning a role that is already correct.
>   **What the marker does NOT do is make the retained `LotActionPermissionDelta` a no-op**, and rev 4 said it did. In the lane's own seeder the **enforcement-enabled branch runs first** (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:42-49`, invoking the delta at `:43`) and **returns before** the marker preservation branch at `:51-54` is ever reached; the delta itself has no marker early return and will happily re-create old permission names (`lane/…/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:65-73`). So on a marked tenant with `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE=true`, running `tenants:seed RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` after wave 1 would still invoke the delta. **What actually makes that harmless is the settled deployment, not the marker:** wave 1 replaces the seeder body with the registry shim (§4.3.4) and nothing in it calls the delta, `PermissionSyncServiceTest::sync_references_no_lot_action_symbol` asserts it, and wave 3 deletes the delta as dead code. The marker's job in this design is exactly one decision — whether a `general_manager` sync creates must also be marked — and claiming more for it was the kind of overstatement that hides a live code path.
> - **What sync still never does.** It never *clears* the marker, never changes its value, never widens it to another role or another source, and never writes it on any role other than a `general_manager` it created in this same statement. On a tenant that **is** marked, step 5 finds the role, adoption has already classified it, and no `provisioning_source` write occurs at all. `PermissionSyncMarkerTest` asserts all five negatives plus the one positive, and `PermissionSyncServiceTest::a_marked_tenant_sees_no_provisioning_source_write` asserts it by query log.

**(4) The `customised_at` writer stays in wave 1, and it is transactional and locked.** Wave 1 already deploys and repeatedly runs sync, so during the soak the existing editor would keep calling `syncPermissions()` without marking customisation (`apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php:223-247`), and the next sync could add template grants to a role the tenant had just edited. **Ruling: the `customised_at` write in `RoleController::update` is a wave-1 deliverable**, in the same lane as sync — set `customised_at = now()` when a system role's permission set actually changes and `customised_at IS NULL`.

**Rev 6, gate r5 B-2 — the writer's transaction is the *same* transaction as its `syncPermissions()`, and it takes the shared lock.** Today that method resolves the role and later calls `syncPermissions()` with **no transaction and no lock at all** (`RoleController.php:223-247` — verified at `971528977`: `Role::findOrFail($id)` at `:225`, `syncPermissions($validated['permissions'])` at `:246`), and rev 5 added a transaction for the audit diff (§4.6.5) without coordinating it with sync. The operator edit therefore becomes: open one transaction → `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)` → `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` the role → compute the audit diff → `syncPermissions()` → `customised_at = now()` → commit. The flag and the grant set it describes are then written or rolled back together, which is what lets §4.3.2 step 6's post-lock re-read be trusted: there is no window in which the grants have changed and the flag has not. **EC-20a** is its test.

**(5) Compatibility mode runs the same adoption.** Adoption is a DB-level classification of `roles` rows, not a template application, so it is safe and necessary in single-schema mode too; leaving every system role at `is_system = false` would leave wave 2's rename/delete protection and both floors with nothing to stand on (§4.6.1, §4.6.3). In compatibility mode sync runs **steps 1–4 only** — rename, create, orphan report and the adoption pass — then stops. **Step 5 is skipped there**: creating a role in a shared `roles` table would provision one tenant's `general_manager` for everybody, and would additionally write a marker claiming, falsely, that every tenant in the schema carries the lane's end state — the exact class of error D1 and the NULL-team ruling exist to prevent. Steps 6–8 are skipped for the same reason (§4.3.3, EC-21).

#### 4.3.1 Command

```php
// apps/api/app/Console/Commands/SyncPermissions.php
final class SyncPermissions extends Command
{
    protected $signature = 'permissions:sync
        {--tenant= : Sync one tenant by id or slug; omit to sync the current tenant context}
        {--dry-run : Compute and report the plan; write nothing}';

    protected $description = 'Synchronise the code-declared permission catalogue into a tenant database.';

    public function __construct(
        private readonly PermissionSyncService $sync,
        private readonly PermissionRegistry $registry,
    ) { parent::__construct(); }

    public function handle(): int;
}
```

Exit codes: `0` applied or already-current, `1` failed (nothing written — the whole run is one transaction), `2` blocked (collision, or compatibility mode with `--tenant`). One marker line per run, on the command output **and** the durable stderr channel, mirroring the shape W-LOT-A-1a established (plan `:1486-1492,1568-1570`):

```
PERMISSIONS-SYNC tenant=<uuid> mode=APPLY|DRY_RUN outcome=APPLIED|ALREADY_CURRENT|BLOCKED|FAILED
                 mutations: renamed=<n> created=<n> adopted_pristine=<n> adopted_customised=<n>
                            roles_created=<n> templates_applied=<n> templates_removed=<n>
                            replacements_granted=<n> admin_changes=<n> marker_written=<n>
                 gauges:    orphans=<n> templates_skipped_customised=<n>
                 reason=<token>
```

> **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 5, gate r4 M-4). The result marker has two kinds of number, and only one of them can be zero on a re-run.** Rev 4 required a second run to report "every counter zero" in five places while two of its counters measure **persistent state** rather than work done: step 3 always reports the current orphan count (a read), and step 6 always counts every skipped customised role. On a tenant with one customised `manager` and one orphan row, rev 4's own design reports `orphans=1 templates_skipped_customised=1` on **every** run forever, so the sentence it repeated five times was false on the first tenant that customised anything — and `:998`'s own gloss ("reported but unchanged") already said so in the same paragraph that demanded zero.
>
> | Kind | Counters | Meaning | Re-run expectation |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | **`mutations`** | `renamed`, `created`, `adopted_pristine`, `adopted_customised`, `roles_created`, `templates_applied`, `templates_removed`, `replacements_granted`, `admin_changes`, `marker_written` | rows this run **wrote** | **all zero**, always |
> | **`gauges`** | `orphans`, `templates_skipped_customised` | state this run **observed** | **unconstrained** — they report whatever is true of the tenant, and a non-zero value is not a re-run failure |
>
> **The idempotency invariant is therefore two clauses, both mechanically checkable: every `mutations` counter is zero AND the run issues zero database writes.** The second is the stronger one and is what every idempotency test asserts (by query-log count); the first exists so a human reading a boot log reaches the same conclusion without a query log. `outcome=ALREADY_CURRENT` is defined as exactly this conjunction, so the marker's outcome token and its numbers cannot disagree.
>
> **Two counters are new in rev 5** and exist because the invariant needs every write represented: **`replacements_granted`** (step 7's deprecation-replacement grants, which rev 4 performed and never counted, so a re-run could write rows while every named counter stayed zero) and **`admin_changes`** (step 8's `syncPermissions()` pivot delta on the `admin` role, which rev 4 called `admin_added` and which must count **removals** too now that `admin = activeKeys()` is a full replace). `admin_added` is withdrawn; `deprecated=<n>` is withdrawn as well, because it counted definitions rather than writes and duplicated `templates_removed`.

**Rev 4 replaces the two `template_migrations_*` counters** — the machinery they measured is gone (§4.3.0a) — with three that measure what sync now actually does: **`templates_removed`**, the keys revoked from uncustomised template-linked roles by step 6's exact diff — deprecated or active (§4.3.2 step 6, the M-4 ruling as widened in rev 5) — which must be visible in a boot log because it is the only revocation sync performs; **`roles_created`**, the missing template roles sync created itself (step 5); and **`marker_written`**, which is `0` or `1` and says whether this run wrote `provisioning_source` on a `general_manager` it created. A gate reading a boot log can tell a first-sync tenant from a re-sync from these three alone, which is what the retired counters were for.

#### 4.3.2 Service

```php
// apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/PermissionSyncService.php
final readonly class PermissionSyncService
{
    public function __construct(
        private PermissionRegistry $registry,
        private PermissionRegistrar $permissionRegistrar,   // Spatie
        private PermissionRenameMap $renameMap,
        private LegacyRoleBaseline $legacyBaseline,
        private RoleMarkerRepository $roleMarkers,          // the public marker predicate, §4.3.0a rule 3
        private PermissionWriteLock $writeLock,             // the SHARED per-tenant lock, rev 6 B-2
        private EventDispatcher $events,                    // emits RoleSyncedV1, §4.6.5
        private Clock $clock,
    ) {}

    /** Full catalogue sync for one tenant: steps 1–9 below. There is no other public method. */
    public function sync(string $tenantId, bool $write): PermissionSyncResult;
}
```

**`syncRole()` is deleted (rev 4, M-9).** Rev 2 introduced a single-role path so EC-10's *Re-apply template* could "run steps 5 and 7 for one template"; rev 3 kept it. With the template-migration machinery retired (§4.3.0a) there is no step 5 to run for one template, and the remaining single-role work is **exactly the step-6 delta for one already-adopted role** — which does not need a second public entry point on the sync service, a second transaction shape, or a second set of counters.

> **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 4). *Re-apply template* is a `RoleController` action over one role, not a sync mode.** `PermissionSyncService` has one public method. The re-apply action, in `RoleController`, inside one transaction: (a) refuse unless the role is `is_system` and carries a `template_key`; (b) set `customised_at = NULL`; (c) call the **same private routine step 6 uses**, extracted as `App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo(Role $role, int $registryVersion): TemplateDelta` (a pure grant-set diff-and-apply over `registry->templateGrants()[$templateKey]`, constructor-injected into both the sync service and the controller, `private readonly`, rule 13); (d) advance `template_version`; (e) emit the diff as `RoleSyncedV1` (§4.6.5). One implementation of "apply a template to a role", two callers — which is what rev 2's `syncRole()` was reaching for and what its signature could not deliver, since `sync(string, bool)` and `syncRole(string, string, bool)` would have been two orderings of the same rules. `TemplateDeltaApplierParityTest` asserts a re-apply and a sync produce byte-identical grant sets for the same role and registry version.

> **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 6, gate r5 B-2). EVERY role-grant writer takes the SAME per-tenant lock, locks the affected `roles` rows, and RE-READS its decision inputs after acquiring it.** Rev 5's D2 safety proof rests entirely on `customised_at` being set by the first operator edit — and then serialized sync against **sync only**, through the advisory lock in the block below. Nothing required the operator edit path, the re-apply path, or wave 0b's `permissions:ensure` to take that lock. A valid interleaving therefore destroyed the very choice `customised_at` exists to protect: (1) sync reads `customised_at IS NULL` and the old grant set; (2) the operator writes a new grant set **and** `customised_at`; (3) sync applies its now-stale **exact** diff (step 6 is a set-equality replace, so it overwrites, it does not merge) and the operator's choice is gone; (4) the role ends `customised_at IS NOT NULL`, so the loss is *disguised as protected customisation* and no counter, banner or audit row says otherwise. Wave 0b has the analogous equality-check-then-grant race before `customised_at` even exists.
>
> **One helper, one lock, taken by every writer of a role↔permission or principal↔role grant.**
>
> ```php
> // apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Infrastructure/Locks/PermissionWriteLock.php
> final readonly class PermissionWriteLock
> {
>     /**
>      * Serialises every role-grant writer for one tenant, for the life of the
>      * CURRENT transaction. MUST be called inside an open transaction.
>      *
>      * PostgreSQL: SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('permissions:' || :tenantId))
>      * SQLite:     no-op (the fast lane is single-connection; the property is
>      *             asserted on the PG lane only — EC-20a/EC-20b).
>      */
>     public function acquire(string $tenantId): void;
> }
> ```
>
> **The five obligations, identical for every writer.** (i) open a transaction; (ii) `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)` as the first statement inside it; (iii) `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` the affected `roles` row(s), in **`roles.id` ascending order** — the same deterministic order EC-19's `users.id` rule uses, and for the same deadlock reason; (iv) **re-read `customised_at` and the role's current grant set after the lock is held**, and make the skip/apply/grant decision from *that* read, never from one taken before; (v) do the write and commit, releasing the advisory lock with the transaction.
>
> | Writer | Section | What the post-lock re-read decides |
> |---|---|---|
> | `PermissionSyncService::sync()` (step 6) | §4.3.2 | whether the role is still `customised_at IS NULL`; the exact diff is computed from the re-read grant set |
> | `TemplateDeltaApplier::applyTo()` — **including the *Re-apply template* caller** | §4.3.2's M-9 ruling, EC-10 | the same; re-apply clears `customised_at` **inside** the lock, so a sync cannot observe the half-state |
> | `permissions:ensure` | §4.3.5 | `matchesPreWave0b()` is evaluated **inside** the lock, once per role, and the batch of additions is written before release |
> | `RoleController::update` (the operator edit) | §4.3.0a rule 4 | **`customised_at = now()` is written in the SAME transaction as its `syncPermissions()`** — rev 5 left the audit-diff transaction (`§4.6.5`) uncoordinated with sync, and a `customised_at` written after a separate `syncPermissions()` commit is a flag that lags the fact it describes |
> | `RoleController::assignRole` / `::removeRole` | §4.1.2's M-1 ruling | the last-admin floor's population, re-counted under the lock rather than before it |
> | `POST` / `DELETE /api/v1/service-accounts/{id}/roles` | §4.1.2's M-1 ruling | the same |
> | `UserController::update`'s `syncRoles()` | §4.6.3 writer 5, EC-4b | the same |
>
> **The lock is per tenant, not per role**, deliberately: sync iterates every role in one transaction, so a per-role lock would have it acquire eight locks in an order another writer need not share. It is a *transaction-scoped advisory* lock rather than a table lock so ordinary reads are never blocked, and `pg_advisory_xact_lock` releases on commit **or rollback**, which is what makes a `FAILED` run safe to retry. `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest` is the mechanical form: a static-analysis assertion that every production method writing `role_has_permissions` or `model_has_roles` — the seven above — reaches `PermissionWriteLock::acquire()` on every path, so an eighth writer added later fails the build rather than racing silently.
>
> **The outcome contract, so a gate can check the behaviour and not only the mechanism: the operator's choice always survives, and sync skips.** Whichever order the two transactions serialize in, the end state is the same — if the operator commits first, sync's post-lock re-read sees `customised_at IS NOT NULL` and skips the role, counting it in the `templates_skipped_customised` **gauge**; if sync commits first, it applies its delta to a then-pristine role and the operator's edit lands afterwards, setting `customised_at` on top. There is no interleaving in which a write is lost, and none in which a role is left `customised_at IS NOT NULL` carrying a grant set the operator did not choose. **Two new edge cases** state it as tests: **EC-20a** *"operator edit vs sync, two PG connections"* and **EC-20b** *"`permissions:ensure` vs operator edit"* (§7, PG lane both — SQLite cannot express two connections and the helper is a no-op there).

Constructor injection only, `private readonly` throughout (rule 13). The team boundary is W-LOT-A-1a's, verbatim in shape:

```php
$previousTeamId = $this->permissionRegistrar->getPermissionsTeamId();
$this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($tenantId);
try {
    return DB::transaction(function () use ($tenantId, $write): PermissionSyncResult {
        $this->writeLock->acquire($tenantId);  // the SHARED PermissionWriteLock, above
        // steps 1..9 below; step 6 SELECTs … FOR UPDATE by roles.id ASC and
        // re-reads customised_at and the grant set AFTER the lock is held
    });
} finally {
    $this->permissionRegistrar->setPermissionsTeamId($previousTeamId);
}
```

**The nine steps, in this order.** Rev 4 re-derives the sequence from the settled ruling in §4.3.0a: **adoption runs before any grant write**, and no step invokes anything from `lane/w-lot-a-1a`. Every step is idempotent; running the command twice in a row produces `ALREADY_CURRENT` — **every `mutations` counter zero and zero database writes**, with the two `gauges` free to report whatever the tenant's state is (§4.3.1's rev-5 M-4 ruling) — re-derived at the end of this list rather than asserted.

1. **Rename.** For each `(from, to)` in the rename map where a `permissions` row named `from` exists and none named `to` exists: `UPDATE permissions SET name = :to WHERE name = :from AND guard_name = 'sanctum'`. **In place** — the primary key never changes, so every `role_has_permissions` and `model_has_permissions` row survives untouched and no grant is lost. If both `from` and `to` exist, the run is `BLOCKED` with `reason=rename_target_exists` (a human must decide which grants win); if neither exists the entry is a no-op and is reported as stale so the map can be trimmed. **A renamed key can never re-enter (rev 4, B-1).** Nothing in sync re-creates a `from` name: step 2 creates only `registry->keys()`, and `PermissionRegistryConsistencyTest::no_manifest_declares_a_rename_source_key` (§4.9.3) proves no manifest declares one. This is precisely the property rev 3's frozen W-LOT input destroyed, and it is restored by removing that input rather than by maintaining a frozen exclusion list — there is **no** "renamed keys must not be re-created" list anywhere in this design, because there is no writer that could create one.
2. **Create.** `Permission::firstOrCreate(['name' => $key, 'guard_name' => 'sanctum'])` for every `registry->keys()`, deprecated keys included — a deprecated permission keeps its row for one release so a custom role referencing it does not break (§4.9).
3. **Orphans.** A `permissions` row present in the database and absent from the registry is **reported, never deleted**. Deleting it would cascade its grants away, and a row can legitimately be a leftover from a tenant that has not yet received a deploy that removed a module. The count appears in the marker and in the `--dry-run` table; wave 3 adds `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` as a separate, deliberate action. **This step writes nothing**, which is why it may sit before adoption.
4. **Adopt** (§4.3.0a rule 2) — **the first step that touches `roles`, and it writes no grant.** For each **existing** role whose name matches a `SystemRoleTemplate` case and whose `template_key IS NULL`, classify its current grant set against that role's version-0 snapshot in `LegacyRoleBaseline` and adopt it as pristine (`customised_at = NULL`) or as customised (`customised_at = now()`), setting `is_system = true`, `template_key` and `template_version = 0`. Counted as `adopted_pristine` / `adopted_customised`, logged per role. A role that already carries a `template_key` is skipped — adoption happens once per role, ever. **Adoption precedes every grant write** (steps 5–8), which is the whole correction: rev 3 ran a role-mutating migration first and could therefore re-add a grant a tenant had removed, making a customised role compare equal and be adopted pristine (B-2).
5. **Create missing template roles.** For each `SystemRoleTemplate` case with no resolvable role in this tenant (NULL-team-tolerant predicate, §4.3.0 clause 3), create it with `tenant_id = :tenantId`, `guard_name = 'sanctum'`, `is_system = true`, `template_key`, `template_version = <registry version>`, `customised_at = NULL`, and **no grants yet** — step 6 grants. Counted as `roles_created`. `general_manager` is created here **like any other template role**, with one addition: when `roleMarkers->tenantCarriesMarker('w-lot-a-1a')` is `false`, the insert also sets `provisioning_source = 'w-lot-a-1a'` and `marker_written` becomes 1 (§4.3.0a rule 3 — the ruling, the CHECK citation and the five negatives). A newly created role is pristine by construction, so it needs no adoption pass.
6. **Template deltas — an EXACT diff on uncustomised template-linked roles.** For each **non-`admin`** template in `registry->templateGrants()`, applied through `TemplateDeltaApplier`:
   - **lock and re-read first (rev 6, gate r5 B-2):** `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` the template-linked roles in **`roles.id` ascending** order, then read `customised_at` and the role's current grant set **from that locked read**. Every decision below is made from it, never from a value read before `PermissionWriteLock::acquire()` — an operator edit that commits between the two is exactly the lost write B-2 describes, and step 6's diff is a set-equality **replace**, so a stale input silently overwrites rather than merging;
   - **skip** the role entirely if the re-read `roles.customised_at IS NOT NULL` — it receives nothing, is counted in the `templates_skipped_customised` **gauge**, and its `template_version` is left where it was, which is what lets the Roles UI say "this role is 3 versions behind" (§4.6);
   - otherwise make the role's grant set **equal the template's set, exactly**: **grant** every key the template declares and the role lacks (counted in `templates_applied`) **and revoke** every key the role holds that the template does not declare (counted in `templates_removed`) — **deprecated or not**;
   - set `template_version` to the registry version on success;
   - emit one **`RoleSyncedV1`** per role whose grant set actually changed, carrying the add/remove diff (§4.6.5).

   > **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 5, gate r4 B-2). `TemplateDeltaApplier` is an exact diff, not an add-plus-deprecation-sweep.** Rev 4 described the applier at §4.3.2 as "a pure grant-set diff-and-apply over `registry->templateGrants()[$templateKey]`" — which promises set equality — and then wrote a step 6 that revoked **only** keys the registry marks deprecated and expressly preserved every *active* key absent from the template. The two cannot both hold, and the settled D2 contract is the first one: a template delta **adds and removes**. Concretely, under rev 4's wording, an active key removed from a later template stayed on a supposedly platform-managed pristine role forever, and *Re-apply template* could never produce the template's set.
   >
   > **Why exact removal is safe here, and only here.** An **uncustomised** template-linked role is one whose grant set has never diverged from its template: `customised_at IS NULL` is written on adoption only when the role compared **equal** to v0 (§4.3.0a rule 2), and it is set to `now()` by `RoleController::update` on the first operator edit (§4.3.0a rule 4). So by construction such a role *is* its template plus whatever sync itself put there, and every key it holds that the template does not declare is a key the platform added and has since retracted — never a tenant's choice. There is therefore nothing for the applier to preserve. Rev 4's stated reason for preserving active keys — *"a pristine role can still legitimately carry a grant an operator added through a path that predates `customised_at`"* — is withdrawn: an operator edit through that path is exactly the case adoption classifies **customised**, because the edit makes the role compare unequal to v0, and a role that compares equal has by definition no such grant.
   >
   > **The three role kinds keep their asymmetry** (§4.9.3's table, restated over the exact diff): an **uncustomised template-linked** role is brought to set equality with its template; a **customised** template-linked role receives nothing in either direction; a **custom** role is never a delta target and loses nothing, its single audited exception being a `replacedBy` grant (step 7). `TemplateDeltaApplierExactnessTest::an_uncustomised_role_equals_its_template_after_apply` asserts set equality in both directions on a role seeded with one extra active key and one missing key, and `::a_customised_role_is_byte_identical_after_apply` asserts the other half by query-log count.
7. **Deprecation replacement grants** — the one case in which sync writes a **custom** role. For every deprecated definition that names a `replacedBy`, grant the successor to every role currently holding the deprecated key, custom roles included, and emit a `RoleSyncedV1` per affected role. A deprecation with `replacedBy: null` writes nothing anywhere.

   > **RULING (orchestrator, rev 3 — M-6, re-anchored in rev 4). Two distinct paths, and only one of them writes a custom role.**
   >
   > - **An in-place rename (step 1) writes NO role at all.** `UPDATE permissions SET name = :to` changes the row's `name`; the primary key is untouched, so every `role_has_permissions` and `model_has_permissions` pivot survives byte-identically and a custom role keeps its grant with **zero writes to `roles` or to any pivot**. This is why the rename map is the preferred mechanism and why EC-6 asserts pivot row counts rather than absence of error.
   > - **A deprecation WITH a `replacedBy` grants the replacement to every role holding the old key, custom roles included.** A deprecation is not a rename: the old key stops being granted by any template and will eventually be pruned, so a custom role that is not given the successor loses the capability on prune day, silently, with no operator decision — the precise outcome D2's "no tenant loses a capability without a human decision" forbids. So sync writes one `role_has_permissions` row per affected custom role, and **each such write emits a `RoleSyncedV1` audit event** (§4.6.5) naming the role, the deprecated key and the replacement, so the exception is visible in the audit chain rather than being an invisible carve-out.
   >
   > This is stated as an **invariant in §4.3, not only here**, because "custom roles are never written" appears three times in this document and a reader must meet the exception wherever they meet the rule. `PermissionsSyncTemplateDeltaTest::custom_roles_receive_only_replacement_grants_and_nothing_else` is the mechanical form: it asserts a custom role's grant set after a sync differs from its grant set before **only** by replacement keys, and that a `RoleSyncedV1` row exists for each. **A custom role is never a delta target and never loses a key of any kind** — step 6's exact diff is scoped by `is_system = true AND template_key IS NOT NULL AND customised_at IS NULL`.
8. **Admin.** The role whose `template_key = 'admin'` is set to exactly `registry->activeKeys()` with `syncPermissions()`. This is the one place a full replace is correct, and it is what replaces `Permission::all()` (`apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:568`, I-19) — after this, the catalogue is once again the boundary of what `admin` holds, and a stray row inserted out of band is no longer silently attached. It runs after step 2 so every active key exists, and after adoption so the role is already `is_system`.
9. **Cache — after commit.** A `finally` inside the `DB::transaction` callback executes **before** the callback returns and therefore before Laravel commits, leaving a window in which another request rebuilds and re-caches the *old* snapshot which then outlives the commit. The flush is registered as an **after-commit hook** — `DB::afterCommit(fn () => $this->permissionRegistrar->forgetCachedPermissions())` inside the transaction, so it fires once the commit has actually landed — and it runs **while the permissions team id is still set to this tenant**, because the S-1 key scheme (§4.8) derives the cache namespace from it. Concretely, the ordering inside `sync()` is: `setPermissionsTeamId($tenantId)` → `DB::transaction(… ; DB::afterCommit($flush))` → `finally { setPermissionsTeamId($previous) }`, so the hook has fired before the team id is restored. `PermissionsSyncTest::the_cache_is_flushed_after_commit_not_before` asserts it by observing a concurrent read on a second connection.

> **Why the second run is `ALREADY_CURRENT` with every MUTATION counter zero and no write — re-derived, not asserted (rev 4 B-1, corrected in rev 5 for the gauge split, gate r4 M-4).** Step by step, on a tenant the first run has completed: **1** every `from` is gone and nothing re-created it, so `renamed=0`; **2** every `registry->keys()` row exists, so `firstOrCreate` inserts nothing and `created=0`; **3** the orphan set is a **read**, and its count is a **gauge** — it stays whatever it was and is not required to be zero; **4** every template-named role now carries a `template_key`, so adoption skips all of them and `adopted_*=0`; **5** every `SystemRoleTemplate` case resolves, so `roles_created=0` and `marker_written=0`; **6** every uncustomised template role's grant set already **equals** its template, so `templates_applied=0` and `templates_removed=0`, while `templates_skipped_customised` is a **gauge** reporting the same non-zero number as the first run whenever the tenant has a customised role; **7** every role holding a deprecated key already holds its successor, so `replacements_granted=0`; **8** `syncPermissions(activeKeys())` on a role that already equals that set writes no pivot row, so `admin_changes=0`; **9** the flush is unconditional and is not a counter. The claim is therefore a consequence of the nine steps rather than a promise laid over them, and `PermissionsSyncTest::a_second_run_writes_no_row` asserts **zero write statements by query-log count** — the invariant's stronger clause — plus every `mutations` counter zero, and deliberately asserts **nothing** about the two gauges.

`--dry-run` runs steps 1–8 with `$write = false` inside a transaction that is rolled back, and prints the plan as a table. It is the only supported way to preview a deploy's authorization delta.

> **§4.3 invariants, restated in one place so they can be cited as a set.** (i) `provisioning_source` is **never cleared, changed or widened** by sync, and is **written in exactly one case**: on a `general_manager` sync itself creates for an unmarked tenant (step 5, §4.3.0a rule 3). (ii) `tenant_id`, `name` and `guard_name` are never written on a role that already exists; sync writes them only on a row it creates itself. (iii) A **customised** system role (`customised_at IS NOT NULL`) receives no template delta — neither a grant nor a revocation. (iv) A **custom** role (`is_system = false`) receives **no template grant, ever**, and never loses a key of any kind — the sole exception being a `replacedBy` grant on a deprecation, logged as a `RoleSyncedV1` audit event (step 7). (v) **Sync references no symbol from `lane/w-lot-a-1a`** — no delta, no activation flag, no frozen input list (§4.3.0a). (vi) **Every role-grant writer — sync, `TemplateDeltaApplier` including re-apply, `permissions:ensure`, `RoleController::update`, `RoleController::assignRole`/`removeRole`, the service-account role routes and `UserController::update`'s `syncRoles` — takes the same `PermissionWriteLock`, locks its `roles` rows by `roles.id` ASC, and re-reads `customised_at` and the grant set after acquiring it** (rev 6, gate r5 B-2). Every one of the six is asserted by a named test in wave 1.

#### 4.3.3 Where it runs

**Replacing the destructive boot reseed.** The relevant range is `apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:148-170`, not the flag block alone (rev 5, gate r4 citation audit): `:148-154` runs `tenants:migrate-rolling --force` **unconditionally**, and only then does `:162-170` run `tenants:seed --class='Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder'` across every tenant whenever `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT=true` — which staging sets, so every push silently reverts every tenant's customisation of the seven seeded roles (I-20, verified `docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/07-verification-opus.md:188`). That the migration line is unconditional and comes first is what makes the B-3 ruling below necessary. The `:162-170` block is replaced by:

```sh
# The status directory is created with the other runtime dirs at the top of the
# script, beside the existing `mkdir -p /var/run` (apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:19-23).
mkdir -p /var/run/autoerp
chmod 755 /var/run/autoerp

if [ "$SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT" = "true" ]; then
  if DB_HOST="$DIRECT_DB_HOST" php artisan permissions:sync-fleet; then
    echo "  Permission sync: [all tenants applied or already current]"
    # SUCCESS OVERWRITES the file. `>` truncates, so a recovered deploy clears a
    # previous boot's failure marker; rev 2 wrote only the failure arm, so a
    # container that recovered stayed permanently unhealthy.
    printf '%s\n' "permissions_sync=ok" > /var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status
  else
    echo "  Permission sync: [FAILED - see PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET marker]"
    printf '%s\n' "permissions_sync=failed" > /var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status
  fi
fi
```

**The file has a named reader, and a named endpoint (rev 3, B-8).** Rev 2 said "`/health` reports `permissions_sync: failed`" without saying which `/health`, and the repository has two with different audiences:

| Endpoint | Route | Handler | Today |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/health` | `apps/api/routes/api.php:27`, **public, no auth** | `MonitoringController::ping` (`apps/api/app/Modules/Admin/Presentation/Controllers/MonitoringController.php:28-36`) | returns `{status, timestamp}` and nothing else |
| `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` | `apps/api/routes/api.php:83`, inside the group gated `['auth:sanctum-admin', 'super_admin', 'throttle:admin-sensitive']` (`:57-58`) | `MonitoringController::health` (`:42-45`) → `HealthCheckService::check()` | returns `database`, `redis`, `queue`, `storage` checks (`apps/api/app/Modules/Admin/Application/Services/HealthCheckService.php:19-33`) |

> **Ruling.** The reader is **`HealthCheckService`**, which gains a fifth check `permissions_sync` in the array at `HealthCheckService.php:19-24`. It reads `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status` and returns `['healthy' => true]` when the file is absent (a container that never ran a boot sync is not unhealthy) or contains `permissions_sync=ok`, and `['healthy' => false, 'error' => 'permission sync reported failure on last boot']` when it contains `permissions_sync=failed`. Because `check()` derives `$allHealthy` from every check (`:26`), a failed sync also flips the aggregate — which is the point.
>
> The key is therefore exposed on the **authenticated `/v1/admin/monitoring/health`**, and the **public `/v1/health` is unchanged**: it stays a two-field liveness probe for the load balancer (`MonitoringController::ping`), because a public endpoint must not disclose the platform's internal authorization state, and because flipping it to 503 on a permission-sync failure would take the whole fleet out of the load balancer over a partial authorization problem — the opposite of "boot continues".
>
> **Deploy checklist reads the admin endpoint**, not the public one, and the Dokploy log line to grep for is the aggregate marker **`PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET`** emitted by the fleet runner (the `docker logs` line named in the wave-1 deploy list, §8), which names every blocked or failed tenant id.

**`permissions:sync-fleet` — a real fleet runner, because `tenants:run` cannot be one (rev 2, B-5).** Rev 1 proposed `php artisan tenants:run permissions:sync --force`. Three things were wrong with it:

1. **The option shape is invalid.** Under `tenants:run`, child options must be forwarded as `--option=`; a bare child flag errors. The repo documents this in the command it copied the shape from: *"The bare `--dry-run` flag is NOT valid under `tenants:run` (it errors); pass options with the `--option=` form"* (`apps/api/app/Console/Commands/SeedChartsCommand.php:31-36`), and W-LOT-A-1a proves the working shape as `--option=['apply=1']` (plan `:1573-1601,1941-1942`).
2. **`--force` does not exist** on `permissions:sync` (§4.3.1) and never did. It is **removed**, not defined: a sync that is additive, template-delta and idempotent has nothing for a force flag to override, and W-LOT-A-1a's tenth guard rail is literally "no force option exists" (plan `:1409-1420`).
3. **`tenants:run` discards child exit statuses** (plan `:2956`), and the entrypoint additionally catches migration and seed failures and keeps booting (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:150-169`). A half-synced fleet would be logged as completed while the app started serving mixed authorization state.

So the fleet runner is its own command, iterating tenants itself rather than delegating to `tenants:run`:

```php
// apps/api/app/Console/Commands/SyncPermissionsFleet.php
protected $signature = 'permissions:sync-fleet
    {--tenants=* : limit to these tenant ids or slugs; omit for every tenant}
    {--dry-run   : compute and report every tenant plan; write nothing}';
```

Its contract:

- it iterates the tenant directory, and for each tenant initialises tenancy and calls `PermissionSyncService::sync()` **directly**, so it holds the real `PermissionSyncResult` rather than a discarded exit code;
- **per-tenant failure isolation**: one tenant's `FAILED`/`BLOCKED` does not stop the run — every remaining tenant is still attempted;
- it emits one `PERMISSIONS-SYNC` marker line per tenant (§4.3.1 shape) plus a closing aggregate line `PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET tenants=<n> applied=<n> already_current=<n> blocked=<n> failed=<n> gauges.provisioned_during_run=<n>` listing every non-green tenant id explicitly;
- **it snapshots the tenant directory once, at the start, and skips any tenant whose database was created after the run began (rev 6, gate r5 m-7).** Such a tenant has already been synced by provisioning (trigger 1), so skipping it is not an omission; the count is reported as the **gauge** `provisioned_during_run` so an operator can see it happened without the number ever failing an idempotency assertion. Should both triggers nonetheless reach one tenant — the fleet iterator is processing it as provisioning commits — they serialize on the shared `PermissionWriteLock` and the second reports `ALREADY_CURRENT` with every `mutations` counter zero. **EC-37** is its test;
- **it exits non-zero if any tenant is `BLOCKED` or `FAILED`** — this is the property `tenants:run` cannot provide and the reason the command exists;
- `--dry-run` reports the fleet-wide plan and always exits 0 unless a tenant is unreachable.

**The entrypoint keeps booting** — refusing to start the container on a permission-sync failure would turn a partial authorization problem into a total outage — but it stops lying about it: it writes a structured marker on **both** arms, and **`GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` reports `permissions_sync: {healthy: false}`** (a fifth key beside `database`, `redis`, `queue`, `storage`) until a subsequent successful run **overwrites** the file with `permissions_sync=ok`. That makes a partial fleet sync a visible, alertable state rather than a line in a boot log nobody reads. **Deploy checklist**: a new row *"after deploy, confirm `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` shows `permissions_sync.healthy = true`; if not, grep the Dokploy container log for the `PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET` aggregate line, which names every blocked or failed tenant id, then run `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet` for those tenants"* is added to the runbook alongside the existing post-deploy steps, and to §8's wave-1 deploy list.

The environment variable name is **kept** — W-LOT-A-1a's §11 evidence protocol asserts its value on staging in six places (plan `:2643,3043,3164,3755,4112`) and its deployment-variable table row at `:2367`; renaming it would invalidate that lane's evidence. What changes is only what the flag runs. The flag's default stays `false` in production until wave 1's staging soak passes (§8).

> **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 5, gate r4 B-3). There is NO `DatabaseMigrated` listener. Sync has exactly two explicit triggers, and a rolling migration is neither of them.**
>
> Rev 4 specified a flag-controlled `permissions:sync-fleet` block **and**, two paragraphs later, an unconditional listener on Stancl's tenant-migrated event. The two cannot coexist: `tenants:migrate` dispatches `DatabaseMigrated` once per tenant on every invocation, including a no-op one (`apps/api/vendor/stancl/tenancy/src/Commands/Migrate.php:51-60`, the dispatch at `:59`; pinned `stancl/tenancy` **v3.10.0**, `apps/api/composer.lock:8242-8243`), and `RollingTenantMigrationCommand` calls `tenants:migrate` once per tenant (`apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Commands/RollingTenantMigrationCommand.php:80-101`, the call at `:121`) from an entrypoint line that is **unconditional** and runs **before** the flagged block (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:148-154`, the flagged block at `:162-170`). So with the flag `false` every rolling-migrated tenant would still be synced through the listener, and with it `true` every tenant would be synced **twice**; and neither listener run would be represented by `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status`, whose two arms exist only inside the flag block. OQ-4 would then be a question about a flag that does not govern the behaviour it names.
>
> **The two triggers, and nothing else:**
>
> 1. **Provisioning — `TenantInitializationService` calls `PermissionSyncService::sync()` directly.** Its `seedRolesAndPermissionsIfMissing()` (`apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenantInitializationService.php:199-217`, called at `:75`) is **replaced** in wave 1 by a constructor-injected `PermissionSyncService::sync($tenantId, write: true)` — no `Artisan::call('db:seed')`, no "if admin exists" guard, because sync is idempotent and needs no guard. The call keeps its position at `:75`, i.e. before `assignDefaultRoles()` at `:77` assigns `admin`, so the role exists when it is assigned. This is the **only** provisioning trigger, and `TenantProvisioningSyncTest` asserts a freshly provisioned tenant holds `registry->activeKeys()` on `admin` with no boot flag set and no migration event involved.
> 2. **Deploy — `permissions:sync-fleet` in the entrypoint, under `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT`.** The block quoted above, and nothing else. **OQ-4 governs it completely**, which is the property that makes OQ-4 answerable.
>
> **Rolling migrations never trigger sync.** `tenants:migrate-rolling` continues to do exactly what it does today — schema only. The consequence is stated rather than hidden: with `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT=false`, a deploy that adds a permission migrates every tenant's schema and syncs none of them, and the new key reaches tenants only when an operator runs `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet` as the named deploy step (§8, wave 1). That is precisely the trade OQ-4 puts to the owner, and it is now a real trade rather than one a listener silently pre-empted.
>
> `PermissionSyncTriggerCensusTest` is the mechanical form: a static-analysis assertion that no production listener, subscriber or service-provider binding references `Stancl\Tenancy\Events\DatabaseMigrated`, and that the only production callers of `PermissionSyncService::sync()` are `TenantInitializationService`, `SyncPermissions` (the command) and `SyncPermissionsFleet`.

**Status marker and admin health describe the deploy trigger only.** `/var/run/autoerp/permissions-sync.status` is written by the entrypoint block and by nothing else, so `HealthCheckService`'s `permissions_sync` check answers exactly one question — *did this container's boot fleet sync succeed* — and answers "healthy" when the file is absent, which now unambiguously means "this container ran no boot sync". A provisioning-time sync failure surfaces where every other provisioning failure does: as a failed tenant creation, not as a fleet health key.

**Provisioning, restated.** A fresh tenant gets the catalogue through `PermissionSyncService::sync()`, the same service the fleet runner calls — one write path, per convention 11. Rev 4 said `TenantInitializationService` "keeps calling `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`" and that the seeder delegates (§4.3.4); the shim survives for its **static** helpers and its `--class` string (the exporter, the staging protocol), but the provisioning path calls the service directly rather than round-tripping through `Artisan::call('db:seed')` inside a request.

**Compatibility mode.** In single-schema compatibility mode every tenant shares one `permissions` table (S-1's own scoping rationale, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-request-hygiene-phase-a.md:66`). Steps 1–3 (rename, create, orphan report) are tenant-independent and safe, and so is step 4 (adoption), which writes no grant; steps 5–8 are not, because the `roles` rows are shared. So: with `TENANCY_DB_PER_TENANT=false`, `permissions:sync` **refuses `--tenant`** (exit 2, `reason=compatibility_mode_is_global`) and runs **steps 1–4 only**, once globally, reporting `templates_applied=0 templates_removed=0 roles_created=0 marker_written=0`. **Adoption runs here too:** it is a DB-level classification of the shared `roles` rows, not a template application, and skipping it would leave every system role at `is_system = false`, which is exactly the state wave 2's rename/delete protection and the two floors depend on (§4.6.1, §4.6.3). A compatibility-mode tenant would otherwise reach wave 2 with an unprotected `admin` role. **Step 5 in particular must not run there**: creating one tenant's `general_manager` in a shared `roles` table provisions it for everybody — the exact class of error D1 and the NULL-team ruling exist to prevent — and writing the marker there would additionally claim, falsely, that every tenant in the schema carries the lane's end state. This is stated rather than silently degraded because a silent degrade is exactly how I-20 happened.

**Compatibility mode and the template-key uniqueness (rev 3, B-7).** Because the shared `roles` table legitimately holds one `manager` row per tenant — the existing Spatie unique is `(tenant_id, name, guard_name)` (`apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2025_11_29_231806_create_permission_tables.php:43-47`) — adoption there necessarily writes `template_key = 'manager'` on **several** rows. That is why §5.2's uniqueness is `unique(tenant_id, template_key)` plus a partial unique on `template_key` alone for NULL-team legacy rows, and why EC-21's test provisions **two** tenants rather than one.

#### 4.3.4 `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` becomes a thin adapter

The class name, namespace and public static signatures are **preserved**. **Rev 2 corrects the census from "five call sites" to the full PHP dependency list** (minor 2): the entrypoint `--class` string (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:165`), two staging `docker exec` heredocs in W-LOT-A-1a's evidence protocol (plan `:2814`), W-LOT-A-1a's mandatory PG preservation test named after it (`RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest`, plan `:1931-1932`), **and seven PHP references**: `apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ExportFrontendPermissionsMap.php:29-30`, `apps/api/database/seeders/DatabaseSeeder.php:76`, `DemoTenantSeeder.php:111`, `CoffeeShopSeeder.php:116`, `ParapharmacySeeder.php:316`, `ProductionSeeder.php:70`, and `apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/Application/Services/TenantInitializationService.php:213-216` — **which wave 1 deletes rather than re-points**, because the provisioning trigger becomes a direct `PermissionSyncService::sync()` call (§4.3.3's rev-5 B-3 ruling); the other six are re-pointed or verified. Wave 1's plan must dispose of each of the seven, not five. W-LOT-A-1a never states a preservation rule for the name; **this spec states it**, so that a later lane cannot rename the class and quietly break the staging protocol.

After wave 1 the class is:

```php
final class RolesAndPermissionsSeeder extends Seeder
{
    public function __construct(
        private readonly PermissionSyncService $sync,
        private readonly PermissionRegistry $registry,
        private readonly TenancyResolver $tenancy,
    ) {}

    public function run(): void;                         // → $this->sync->sync($tenantId, write: true)

    /** @return list<string> */
    public static function permissionNames(): array;     // → app(PermissionRegistry::class)->keys()

    /** @return array<string, list<string>> */
    public static function rolePermissionGrants(): array; // → app(PermissionRegistry::class)->templateGrants()
}
```

The two static helpers are the one sanctioned exception to rule 13's "never use `app()`": they are static, so no constructor exists to inject into, and they exist purely to keep the exporter's and W-LOT-A-1a's contracts intact. They are marked with an explicit `@internal Compatibility shim — call PermissionRegistry directly in new code.` docblock, and the PHPStan rule in §4.5 lists them among the call sites that may reference permission strings. Wave 3 migrates the exporter to the registry directly and deletes both.

The 871-line array (`apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:48-871`) is deleted in the same commit that adds the manifests, and `PermissionCatalogueParityTest` — a **transitional** test that pins the registry's key set against a frozen snapshot of the 304 pre-existing keys plus the declared additions and minus the declared renames — proves the move loses nothing. That test is deleted at the end of wave 1; it exists only to make the cut-over reviewable.

#### 4.3.5 `permissions:ensure` — the wave-0b stopgap, and why it is needed at all

> **BLOCKER found by gate r2 (B-4), ruled by the orchestrator 2026-09-10.** Wave 0b declares roughly twenty new permission keys and gates live routes on them, and rev 2 deployed them with `php artisan tenants:seed --class=…RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` — the sequence the T-2/T-3 spec documents. **After `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merges that sequence writes nothing on a marked tenant.** `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE` defaults to **false** (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/config/lot_action_permissions.php:5`), and with enforcement off the incoming seeder takes the `markedTenantCarriesWlota1aDelta()` branch and returns having written nothing at all (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:51-54`, predicate at `:71-76`) — which is *correct* behaviour for that lane, whose whole point is that a marked tenant is never re-seeded. The consequence for wave 0b is that a marked tenant would not receive `credit-notes.cancel`, `services.*`, `channels.*` or any other 0b key while its routes are already gated on them, and Spatie fails closed: **403 for everyone, including `admin`** — the exact `credit-notes.cancel` failure mode 0b exists to fix, reproduced on a fresh set of keys.

Wave 0b therefore provisions its own keys through a small, additive command of its own, and **does not rely on `tenants:seed` at all**:

```php
// apps/api/app/Console/Commands/EnsurePermissions.php
protected $signature = 'permissions:ensure
    {--keys=*     : permission keys to create if absent}
    {--grant-to=* : role names that must hold every named key (in addition to admin)}
    {--dry-run    : report the plan; write nothing}';
```

Its contract is deliberately the smallest thing that closes the hole:

- **Creates only.** `Permission::firstOrCreate(['name' => $key, 'guard_name' => 'sanctum'])` per key. It never renames, never deprecates, never deletes.
- **Grants to `admin` unconditionally, and to a named template role only when that role is provably untouched** — the rev-4 ruling below. It never revokes anything from any role, so it cannot undo a tenant customisation and cannot undo the lane's `manager` recall revocation.
- **Safe on a marked tenant.** It reads the marker only through `RoleMarkerRepository` and **writes no marker**, because it touches neither `provisioning_source` nor `general_manager`'s existence — it only adds permission rows and grant pivots. That is why it is safe to run where `tenants:seed` deliberately no-ops.
- **Serialized against every other role-grant writer (rev 6, gate r5 B-2).** The whole per-tenant run is one transaction that takes **`PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)`** as its first statement, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`s the named `--grant-to` roles in `roles.id` ascending order, and evaluates `matchesPreWave0b()` **from the post-lock re-read**. Wave 0b has no `customised_at` column yet, so equality *is* its only guard, and an equality check taken before the lock is the same lost-write race B-2 describes with nothing left to detect it afterwards. **EC-20b** is its test.
- **Idempotent and logged.** A second run reports `created=0 granted=0`. One marker line per tenant: `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE tenant=<uuid> created=<n> granted=<n> admin_only=<comma-list> missing_roles=<comma-list>`, on the command output and the stderr channel, the same shape as the sync marker (§4.3.1).
- **Runs per tenant through the same fleet mechanism** the entrypoint already uses for migrations, so it inherits per-tenant failure isolation.

> **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 4, B-4). `--grant-to` is equality-guarded; `admin` is not.** Rev 3 granted every requested key to every named `--grant-to` role unconditionally, arguing that additive writes are safe. They are not: granting a **new capability** to a role a tenant has customised is exactly the widening D2 forbids (`§0` D2), and the wave-1 `customised_at` column does not exist yet in wave 0b, so the command had no way to tell a customised role from a pristine one. It now has one:
>
> 1. **`admin` always.** The registry-era rule is `admin = activeKeys()` (§4.3.2 step 8) and `admin` is not operator-editable at all (Floor F-1, §4.6.2), so granting a new key to `admin` restores an invariant rather than widening a choice. No guard.
> 2. **A named `--grant-to` role only when its CURRENT grant set equals its frozen PRE-0b baseline for that role** — which is **`postA1aGrants(role)`, not `version0(role)`.**
>
>    > **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 6, gate r5 B-1). `permissions:ensure` uses its OWN named predicate against the post-A-1a map; `matchesVersion0()` is the wave-1 adoption oracle and is not reused here.** Rev 5 settled v0 as `postA1a ∪ wave0bAdditions` (§4.3.0a rule 1) — which is correct for adoption — and then had `ensure` call `matchesVersion0()` *before granting the additions*. That is unsatisfiable by construction: on a pristine post-A-1a `manager`, `current = P` while `version0 = P ∪ A`, so `matchesVersion0(P)` is **false**, the role is classified customised, and it receives **none** of `A`. The very first wave-0b grant would have been unreachable, falsifying "a pristine manager receives the key", `adopted_customised=0`, EC-32c's pristine-manager result and the change-log's claim that r4's B-1 was closed. The two predicates measure different moments and must be two named functions:
>    >
>    > | Predicate | Home | Compares | Used by | True when |
>    > |---|---|---|---|---|
>    > | **`matchesPreWave0b(string $templateKey, array $currentKeys): bool`** *(new, wave 0b)* | `LegacyRoleBaseline` | `canonical($currentKeys) == canonical(postA1aGrants($templateKey))` | `permissions:ensure` **only** | the role is still exactly what `lane/w-lot-a-1a` left it — i.e. it has not been touched **before** 0b runs |
>    > | `matchesVersion0(string $templateKey, array $currentKeys): bool` *(wave 0b, consumed in wave 1)* | `LegacyRoleBaseline` | `canonical($currentKeys) == canonical(version0($templateKey))`, `version0 = postA1aGrants ∪ wave0bAdditions` | wave 1 **adoption** (§4.3.0a rules 1–2) **only** | the role is what the lane left it **plus** whatever 0b's equality guard legitimately granted it |
>    >
>    > Both are pure functions over arrays, both normalise through `PermissionRenameMap` on **both** sides, and neither depends on the wave-1 `roles` columns — no `is_system`, no `template_key`, no `customised_at`. That is why `LegacyRoleBaseline` ships in **wave 0b**, one wave earlier than rev 3 placed it, and why wave 1 inherits it rather than introducing it. The two are consistent by construction and that consistency is the *reason* v0 may include the additions: a role that `ensure` granted was `matchesPreWave0b`-true beforehand, so afterwards it is `matchesVersion0`-true; a role `ensure` skipped was already unequal for a reason predating 0b and stays unequal. `LegacyRoleBaselinePredicateTest` asserts the pair does not collapse: on a pristine post-A-1a `manager`, `matchesPreWave0b` is **true** and `matchesVersion0` is **false**; after `ensure` grants the seven additions, `matchesPreWave0b` is **false** and `matchesVersion0` is **true**.
>    >
>    > **Eligibility is snapshotted once per role, before any of that role's additions are written.** `ensure` evaluates `matchesPreWave0b` **once per named `--grant-to` role**, at the start of the run and **before writing any grant for that role**, and then grants the approved additions for that role as **one batch** — inside the shared lock of §4.3.5's serialization clause below. Evaluating per key would be the same defect at a smaller scale: the first granted key makes the role unequal, and every subsequent key in the same invocation would be refused. `EnsurePermissionsCustomisationGuardTest::all_seven_additions_land_on_a_pristine_manager_in_one_run` is the mechanical form — one invocation carrying all seven keys, asserting all seven pivot rows exist afterwards and that the snapshot was taken once (by query log).
> 3. **Otherwise: admin only, and say so.** The key is created and granted to `admin`; the named role is **skipped**, listed in the marker's `admin_only=` field, and printed as *"role `manager` has been customised in this tenant — `services.update` was granted to `admin` only; grant it deliberately in Settings → Roles, or it will arrive with the wave-1 *Re-apply template* action."* A skip is **not** a failure: the run still exits 0, because a tenant that customised its `manager` choosing not to receive a new key is the correct outcome, not an incident.
> 4. **A role named in `--grant-to` that does not exist** is reported in `missing_roles=` and skipped, as before. `general_manager` on an unmarked tenant is the common case and is not created here — 0b creates no role.
>
> `EnsurePermissionsCustomisationGuardTest` (PG, two tenants) asserts all four, **against `matchesPreWave0b`** (rev 6, gate r5 B-1 — asserting against `matchesVersion0` here is the defect that ruling names, and would red on the first case): a pristine post-A-1a `manager` **receives the key**; a `manager` with one extra grant and a `manager` with one grant removed both receive nothing and appear in `admin_only=`; `admin` receives it in every case; and the exit code is 0 throughout. `::all_seven_additions_land_on_a_pristine_manager_in_one_run` adds the snapshot-once half.

> **It is explicitly a pre-wave-1 stopgap.** `permissions:ensure` exists only because wave 0b ships live route gates before the registry does. `permissions:sync` **supersedes it entirely** — sync's step 2 creates every registry key and step 6 applies template grants, a strict superset of what this command does. Wave 1's deliverable list therefore includes **deleting `EnsurePermissions.php` and its deploy rows** in the same commit that lands `permissions:sync`.
>
> **`PermissionsSyncSupersedesEnsureTest` asserts the right thing (rev 4, B-4).** Rev 3 claimed the first wave-1 sync on an `ensure`-provisioned tenant reports `ALREADY_CURRENT` with every counter zero. That is impossible and would have shipped a red test: the **first** sync of any tenant is also its **first adoption**, so it must report non-zero `adopted_pristine` / `adopted_customised` (§4.3.2 step 4) — that is the step existing precisely because no earlier command ever wrote `template_key`. The corrected assertions, in order:
>
> **Rev 5 corrects the `renamed` expectation, which was as impossible as the assertion it replaced (gate r4, B-1).** `permissions:ensure` **never renames** — its contract is "creates only" (the first bullet above) — so it leaves every rename **source** row exactly where the legacy catalogue put it, and the first `permissions:sync` of that tenant must therefore rename all of them. Expecting `renamed=0` would have shipped a red test for the same reason rev 3's `ALREADY_CURRENT` did. The expected value is a **known constant**: the rename map has **eleven** entries (§4.9.2), all eleven `from` keys exist in the legacy catalogue that seeded the tenant, and **none** of the eleven `to` keys exists anywhere in that catalogue — verified by grep over the seeder, where each `from` returns ≥1 hit and each `to` returns exactly 0. **The declaration lines, given on BOTH trees because the two differ and rev 5 mixed them (rev 6, gate r5 citation audit — rev 5 printed the *lane's* line numbers under an unqualified `apps/…` path, which reads as HEAD):**

> | Rename source | At HEAD `971528977` | On `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `2fa724c1d` |
> |---|---|---|
> | `uom.edit` | `apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:192` | `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:268` |
> | `deliveries.edit` | `…:212` | `lane/…:288` |
> | `pos_held_orders.view/create/delete` | `…:419-421` | `lane/…:495-497` |
> | `catalog_cart.view/create/convert_po/convert_so/marketplace_checkout/manage_all` | `…:480-485` | `lane/…:556-561` |
>
> Both readings give the same **eleven**, which is why the `renamed=11` expectation is unaffected; what was wrong was the path, not the count. So:
>
> | Run | Expected |
> |---|---|
> | first `permissions:sync` after `permissions:ensure` | `outcome=APPLIED`; **`created=0`** — the mechanical proof that `ensure` left nothing for sync to create; **`renamed=11`**, the full rename map, because `ensure` never renames and every source is present with no target present; `adopted_pristine + adopted_customised = <number of template-named roles in the tenant>`, non-zero, and — under the canonical oracle of §4.3.0a rule 1, **and only because adoption uses `matchesVersion0` while `ensure` used `matchesPreWave0b`** (rev 6, gate r5 B-1) — **`adopted_customised=0` on a tenant whose roles were pristine before `ensure` ran**: the eleven renames are absorbed by `canonical()` on both sides, and `ensure`'s own equality-guarded grants are **inside** `version0` by construction, so neither can make a pristine role compare unequal. Had `ensure` shared adoption's predicate it would have granted nothing at all, and this row's `templates_applied=0` claim would have been false in the opposite direction; `templates_applied=0` for every role `ensure` already granted, and non-zero only for keys `ensure` was never asked for; `templates_removed=0`; `marker_written` is 0 or 1 by whether the tenant was marked |
> | second `permissions:sync` | `outcome=ALREADY_CURRENT` with **every `mutations` counter zero and zero database writes**, asserted by query-log row count (§4.3.2's re-derivation); the two `gauges` are asserted **unchanged from the first run**, not zero (§4.3.1) |
>
> The `created=0` on the first run is the real superseding claim, and it is the one rev 3's wording buried under an assertion that could never have gone green. `renamed=11` is the second half a gate should check: it is the number that proves the two commands have disjoint jobs rather than overlapping ones.

### 4.4 Enforcement standard

#### 4.4.1 The rule

> **E-1.** Every route in `apps/api/routes/api.php` and every module `routes.php` is exactly one of: **gated** (carries `can:`, `require.any.permission:`, `super_admin`, `central_admin*` or `authz.self` middleware), or **public** (declared in the public allow-list). There is no third state.
>
> **E-2.** `can:` route middleware is the **single action gate**. A controller may add defence-in-depth (`Gate::authorize`, `$user->can`) but may not be the *only* gate.
>
> **E-3.** Policies are for **row-level** decisions only — "may this actor touch *this* record" — and are registered explicitly (`apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php:270-276`; there is no `AuthServiceProvider`, and no application-authored `Gate::before` — Spatie's permission callback is the only one registered). A policy never replaces the action gate.
>
> **E-4.** A FormRequest's `authorize()` **must not be the sole permission check**. Of 262 FormRequests, 180 return `true` unconditionally, and eight of the live ungated write routes are "gated" only by such a FormRequest (`docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.md:77-135`) — a shape that reads as authorized in review and is not. `authorize()` may still carry row-level or field-level logic.

Rationale is G15's cautionary tale: Frappe's `@frappe.whitelist()` authorizes nothing by itself and produced CVE-class findings; the defence OWASP prescribes is centralising the check and testing it as a first-class concern. Route middleware is the only layer a mechanical sweep can see, which is what makes E-2 enforceable rather than aspirational.

#### 4.4.2 `authz.self` — the self-service marker

Some authenticated routes legitimately have no permission because the resource *is* the caller. Today they are indistinguishable from a gap: `POST /api/v1/auth/logout`, `logout-all`, `resend-verification`, `POST /api/v1/notifications/{id}/read`, `read-all`, and `POST /api/v1/support-access/sessions/{session}/exit` all appear in the ungated write list (`docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.md:87-95,101-102,127`).

```php
// apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/AllowSelfService.php — alias 'authz.self'
/**
 * Marks a route as acting only on the authenticated principal's own resources.
 * It is a DECLARATION, not a gate: it asserts authentication and records the
 * route as deliberately permission-free so the §4.4.3 ratchet can distinguish
 * "no permission by design" from "no permission by accident".
 */
final class AllowSelfService
{
    public function handle(Request $request, Closure $next): Response;
}
```

Registered in the alias map beside the existing entries (`apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:114-123`). It rejects an unauthenticated request (401) and, for a `PrincipalKind::Service` principal, rejects `logout`-family routes with 403 `SELF_SERVICE_NOT_APPLICABLE` — a machine has no session to end. It performs no ownership check, because ownership on these routes is structural (`$request->user()`), not a lookup; a self-service route that takes an id in the path and looks a record up is **not** eligible for the marker and must carry a policy instead (E-3).

**The marker is not self-certifying (rev 2, B-7).** Rev 1 said "every addition is a reviewed diff on the alias, which is the point" — that is false. The alias is registered **once** in `apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:114-123`; every later use is an ordinary route edit, so a developer could satisfy the ratchet by writing `authz.self` on any ungated route, including one that takes another principal's id. A declaration that a mechanical guard counts as coverage must itself be mechanically constrained. Two tests do that, and **appearing in middleware is never by itself coverage**:

1. **`SelfServiceRouteAllowListTest` holds an exact allow-list of `(method, uri)` pairs** — not a count, not a prefix, the literal seven: `POST api/v1/auth/logout`, `POST api/v1/auth/logout-all`, `POST api/v1/auth/resend-verification`, `GET api/v1/auth/me`, `POST api/v1/notifications/{id}/read`, `POST api/v1/notifications/read-all`, `POST api/v1/support-access/sessions/{session}/exit`. Set equality in **both** directions: a route carrying `authz.self` that is not on the list fails, and a list entry that has lost the marker fails. Adding a route means editing this table, which is a diff a reviewer sees and a gate can argue with.

2. **`SelfServiceRouteShapeTest` is a structural check**: for every marked route, the only permitted URI parameters are the caller's own session or token id (`{session}`, `{tokenId}`) — a route parameter naming any other resource (`{id}` on a record, `{userId}`, `{companyId}`) fails **even if it is on the allow-list**, because a marker route must not be able to address another principal's row. `POST api/v1/notifications/{id}/read` is the one entry with a record id and is therefore **exempted by name with a stated reason**: the notification is looked up scoped to `$request->user()`, so the id selects among the caller's own rows and cannot address another principal's. The exemption is a named constant, not a hole in the rule.

The ratchet (§4.4.3) consults the allow-list, **not** the middleware stack, when classifying a route as self-service. A route wearing `authz.self` without an allow-list entry is classified **uncovered**, so the bypass rev 1 opened is closed at the classifier rather than only at review time.

**Tombstones are exempted by name, not reclassified as public (rev 2, minor 3).** Rev 1 described "four inert tombstones reclassified by the public allow-list". They are not public: all four are authenticated routes returning an unconditional HTTP 410 with no mutation (`docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.md:66-71`) — `POST api/v1/pos/receipts`, `POST api/v1/pos/receipts/{id}/void`, `POST api/v1/pos/receipts/{id}/payments`, `POST api/v1/pos/orders/{id}/close`. Calling them public would be a false statement about their auth stack. They get their own named classification, `TOMBSTONE`, backed by a test asserting each returns 410 unconditionally; a tombstone that ever stops returning 410 fails that test and re-enters the uncovered count.

#### 4.4.3 The route-coverage ratchet

```php
// apps/api/tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php
// baseline: apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/route-permission-coverage-baseline.json
```

Mechanism, following the two ratchets already in the tree (`DocumentPerActionBaselineRatchetTest`, `TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest`):

- **Source of truth is the live router**, not a grep: the test boots the application and reads `Route::getRoutes()`, so a route added by any mechanism is seen. (The audit derived the same data from `php artisan route:list --json`, and Opus re-derived it byte-identically — `docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/07-verification-opus.md:8`.)
- **Classification** per route: gated / public / self-service / tombstone / **uncovered**. A route is **gated** when its resolved middleware stack contains one of `can:`, `require.any.permission:`, `super_admin`, `central_admin`, `central_admin_role`; **public** when it is in the public allow-list; **self-service** when it appears in the §4.4.2 `(method, uri)` allow-list — *not* merely when it wears `authz.self`, so the marker cannot certify itself; **tombstone** when it is one of the four named 410 routes; **uncovered** otherwise.
  > **A FLAG-CONDITIONAL middleware is not a gate (rev 4, verified during the r3 fix round).** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` already attaches `BatchActionAccess:batches.delete` and `BatchActionAccess:batches.recall` to the two batch writes wave 0b was going to gate (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php:27,30`) — but that middleware **returns `$next($request)` with no permission check at all when `LotActionPermissionActivation::enforced()` is false** (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/Middleware/BatchActionAccess.php:17-19`), and `false` is the shipped default (`lane/…/config/lot_action_permissions.php:5`). Counting it as coverage would mark two live, unauthenticated-by-permission writes as gated on every tenant that has not flipped the flag. The classifier's gating list is therefore **exactly the five aliases above and nothing else**, and `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest` carries a named assertion `::a_flag_conditional_middleware_is_not_gating` pinning `BatchActionAccess` as the worked example. This is what 0b-5's *"on top of the lane's middleware, not instead of it"* means concretely: 0b adds a real `can:batches.delete` / `can:batches.recall` **beside** `BatchActionAccess`, so the route is gated unconditionally and the lane's flag keeps governing only the lane's own stricter behaviour.
- **Baseline entry key**: `"{METHODS} {uri}"` — stable across controller refactors and readable in a diff.
- **Three failure directions**, all fail-closed: **growth** (an uncovered route not in the baseline), **stale** (a baseline entry that is now covered — the entry must be deleted in the merge that fixed it, so a fix cannot be forgotten), **anti-growth** (the baseline's key set is compared against a protected blob pinned in CI, exactly as `DocumentPerActionBaselineRatchetTest` does via `DPA_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB`, so a contributor cannot add a violation and its baseline entry in one change).
- **The ratchet measures a stricter thing than the audit's `AUTH_ONLY` count, deliberately.** The audit's 142 are routes with no check at *any* layer. The ratchet counts routes with no *middleware* gate, which is what makes E-2 and E-4 enforceable — a route checked only in a controller or only in a FormRequest is uncovered. Recomputed from `docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.csv` at `971528977`, and **re-derived again in rev 3 with a CSV parser rather than by column split** (minor 1 — rev 2's `638` was simply wrong and made its own arithmetic fail to close): of 1054 data rows, **642 `MIDDLEWARE` + 68 `SUPERADMIN_ONLY` = 710** carry a gating middleware and 18 are `PUBLIC`, leaving **326 uncovered — 177 writes and 149 reads** (writes = any row whose `method` column contains POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE). `642 + 68 + 18 + 326 = 1054` exactly, with no intermediate reclassification needed; rev 2's figure required an unstated transformation and still left four routes unaccounted for (the audit's prose figure of "178 write routes with no route-level middleware" counts one tombstone this classification puts elsewhere). Those 326 decompose as 142 checked nowhere, 130 checked in a controller, 46 in a FormRequest, 8 by a policy.
- **Two ceilings** in the test file: `UNCOVERED_WRITE_CEILING` and `UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING`, both shrink-only. Wave 0a generates the baseline **after** `authz.self` and the public allow-list are applied (0a-3), which reclassifies four inert 410-tombstone writes, six self-service writes and `GET /api/v1/auth/me` — giving `UNCOVERED_WRITE_CEILING = 167` and `UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING = 148`. The two ceilings are separate so that closing reads can never buy headroom for writes — D3's "writes first". **Rev 5 re-derives what wave 0a closes** (gate r4 M-3 moved one item out and B-4 added two): the write closures are Promotion 4, Uom 5, Menu 3, Coupon 3 — **fifteen**, because the counting-item write moved to 0b with the rest of the Inventory routes file — so `167 − 15 = 152`.

> **Rev 6 corrects the READ arithmetic (gate r5 M-2): wave 0a closes SIX reads, not two, so `148 − 6 = 142`.** Rev 5 counted only 0a-5's two coupon reads and forgot that **0a-4 is itself four read closures**: `GET /roles`, `GET /roles/{id}`, `GET /permissions` and `GET /users/{userId}/roles` are all ungated reads today (`apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:59,61,64,78` — verified at `971528977`), and 0a-4 gates every one of them on `roles.view` / `users.assign-roles`. Together with `GET /api/v1/coupons` and `GET /api/v1/coupons/{id}` (`apps/api/app/Modules/Coupon/Presentation/routes.php:12,14`) that is **six**. Leaving the ceiling at 146 would have handed wave 0b four units of unearned read headroom and defeated the shrink schedule the ceiling exists to enforce — the same failure mode in the read direction that D3's "two separate ceilings" rule guards against in the write direction. Wave 0a therefore takes the ceilings to **`UNCOVERED_WRITE_CEILING = 152`** and **`UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING = 142`**, and wave 0b to ~127 writes (the counting-item write it inherits plus its own). *(Rev 3's sixteen and its `167 − 16 = 151` were correct for rev 3's 0a; the write number moves because the task list moved, not because the arithmetic changed.
