| **EC-33** *(new, rev 4 — M-1)* | A central **`SuperAdmin`** authenticates on `auth:sanctum-admin` and reaches a route in the global `api` group, which now carries `EnforceTokenScope` | The middleware is a **strict no-op**: its first statement refuses to act on any subject that is not a tenant `App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User`, so none of its four checks reads `principal_kind`, `status` or memberships off a model that has none (`apps/api/app/Models/SuperAdmin.php:14-32`). The request proceeds unchanged and **no query is issued against the tenant database** | §4.1.4's rev-4 ruling | PG — `EnforceTokenScopeNoOpTest`, a `SuperAdmin` on `GET /api/v1/admin/auth/me` (`apps/api/routes/api.php:47`) and `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` (`:83`); asserts 200 **and a zero-row query log against `user_company_memberships`**, because "it happened to work" and "it did not touch the tenant DB" are different claims |
| **EC-34** *(new, rev 4 — M-2; EXTENDED in rev 5, gate r4 M-1)* | A caller holding `users.update` / `users.delete` / **`users.assign-roles`** but **no** `service-accounts.*` permission targets a **service principal** through the generic user endpoints — update, delete, activate, deactivate, reset-password **and the two role routes `POST` / `DELETE /api/v1/users/{userId}/roles`** (`apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:79-80` → `RoleController::assignRole` `:350-359`, `::removeRole` `:390-398`) | Every one is refused with **422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES`**, naming the `/service-accounts/*` route that does the job; nothing is written, no token is revoked, no membership is removed. `PATCH /users/{id}/pos-pin` keeps its more specific 422 `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_CANNOT_HOLD_PIN` on both arms. `GET /users` and `GET /users/{id}` still **return** the principal, because support must be able to see it exists | §4.1.2's rev-4 ruling and its guard-placement table, plus the rev-5 M-1 ruling for the two role routes | PG — one refusal assertion per endpoint (**seven** — five `UserController` writes plus the two role routes), each asserting the **row is unchanged** (for the role routes: the target's `model_has_roles` rows) rather than the status alone; plus a success assertion that the same caller holding `service-accounts.update` can assign through `POST /api/v1/service-accounts/{id}/roles`; plus the census test failing when a new `UserController` **or `RoleController`** method resolves a row without calling the guard |
| **EC-35** *(new, rev 4 — M-5)* | A **console-originated** deprecation replacement: `permissions:sync` runs on a deploy, a deprecated key names a `replacedBy`, and a custom role holding the old key receives the successor | An audit row lands on `audit_events` from an **`identity.role.synced.v1`** / `RoleSyncedV1` event *(rev 6, gate r5 m-1 — this row said `identity.role.synced`, contradicting §4.6.5's own end-to-end versioning ruling three lines below the class it names)* carrying the add/remove diff and `cause: 'deprecation_replacement'` — **not** a `RoleUpdated`, whose non-null `companyId`/`actorUserId` sync cannot supply (§4.6.5's ruling). The row has `company_id IS NULL`, a populated `tenant_id`, `user_id IS NULL`, and `metadata` carrying neither `principal_id` nor `token_id` (absent, not empty-string) | §4.6.5's `RoleSyncedV1` ruling — the `AuditEvent.php:102` guard change, `AuditService::recordTenantWide()` and the `persistTenantWideEvent()` subscriber branch | PG — `RoleSyncMutationAuditTest` asserts the row **lands and is readable back**, that its `company_id` is null and its `event_hash` is populated (proving the constructor took the real branch rather than the silent empty one), and that no `identity.role.updated` row was written |
| **EC-36** *(new, rev 4 — B-1; counter wording corrected in rev 5, gate r4 M-4)* | A **fresh post-wave-1 tenant** is provisioned, synced, and then synced again on the next deploy | The second run is `ALREADY_CURRENT` with **every `mutations` counter zero and zero row writes**. On a *fresh* tenant both gauges also happen to be zero — there is no orphan and no customised role — but the assertion is over the mutation set and the query log, so the row stays true on a tenant that later customises something. Rev 3's design could not deliver this: its template migration re-created every renamed **source** key from a frozen list containing `uom.edit` (`lane/…:268`), `deliveries.edit` (`:288`), `pos_held_orders.*` (`:495-497`) and `catalog_cart.*` (`:556-561`), assembled at `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:86-90` and created at `lane/…/LotActionPermissionDelta.php:65-73`, so the second run found `from` and `to` both present and was `BLOCKED` with `reason=rename_target_exists` — on a tenant that had never held a legacy key. Retiring the migration (§4.3.0a) removes the only writer that could re-create a rename source, and §4.3.2's step-by-step re-derivation is the proof rather than the promise | §4.3.2 steps 1–9 and its "why the second run is `ALREADY_CURRENT`" derivation | PG — a tenant provisioned **after** wave 1; `permissions:sync` twice; asserts `outcome=ALREADY_CURRENT`, every `mutations` counter zero, **zero write statements in the query log**, and that no `permissions` row named `uom.edit`, `deliveries.edit`, `pos_held_orders.view` or `catalog_cart.view` exists at any point |

Three rows deserve a note because they are the ones most likely to be argued at a gate. **EC-15**, as reversed in rev 2, is the row that shows where orthogonality **stops**: for every ordinary role, action and scope stay independent and assigning a role never widens location access — but `general_manager` is refused outright to a location-restricted principal, because the incoming lane makes an unrestricted membership a precondition of the grant rather than a consequence of it. A gate that can show *any other* role silently widening location access has broken the D1/G5 model; a gate pointing at `general_manager` is pointing at the invariant, not at a bug. **EC-17** is deliberately *not* fixed: closing a five-minute UI staleness window costs a websocket or a poll, and the exposure is rendering an affordance the server will refuse. **EC-31** is the only accepted-residual row; it is a property of offline POS, not of this design, and it is listed so nobody later mistakes it for a regression this lane introduced.
---

## 8. Waves and sequencing

**Laptop lane cap: 3 concurrent lanes** (owner, 2026-09-09). No wave below is planned to run more than two RBAC lanes at once, leaving one slot for whatever else is in flight. Every wave is one execution plan + one Codex adversarial gate on the plan + one reviewer gate on the diff; `tenancy-authz-reviewer` is **mandatory on every wave**, and `frontend-conventions-reviewer` is mandatory on any wave touching `apps/web`.

### Wave 0a — stop the bleeding (starts now; no dependency on any in-flight lane)

Entry condition: **none**. Every item uses permissions that already exist, adds no migration, and — **as re-measured against all three lanes at their current tips, not merely asserted** — touches no file in any of the three diffs **except the single accepted `apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json` ceiling increment ruled on in 0a-1 below**. *(Rev 6, gate r5 m-2: rev 4's unqualified "touches no file in any of the three diffs" survived the rev-4.1 ruling that restored 0a-1 and knowingly accepted that one overlap, so this lead-in contradicted the ruling two screens below it. The re-measurement itself is rev 5's and is re-checked in rev 6 — the three lane SHAs are unchanged, §0.)*

**Rev 2 re-scope (B-9).** Rev 1's unconditional no-overlap claim was false. Two of that lane's files were also claimed by wave 0a, and two more items were never 0a items at all:

| Rev-1 task | Collision | Rev-2 disposition |
|---|---|---|
| 0a-5's BatchExpiry portion (`batches` delete/recall route gating) | the lane rewrites `apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php` wholesale (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:…/routes.php:13-46`, introducing `BatchActionAccess` middleware) | **moved to 0b** |
| 0a-10 glossary rows | the lane adds the General-manager row and the sole-writer sentence to `docs/glossary.md` (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:docs/glossary.md:21,91`) | **moved to 0b** |
| 0a-6 delete `PermissionSeeder.php` | rev 1 itself admitted it must land *after* 0b-1 declares `credit-notes.cancel` — so it was never a wave-0a item | **moved to 0b** |
| 0a-7 fix the masking test | rev 1 itself admitted it leaves the suite red until 0b — a red commit is not independently mergeable | **moved to 0b**, landing in the same commit as 0b-1 |

#### Rev-5 re-measurement and third re-scope (gate r4, M-3)

Rev 2 measured `lane/w-lot-a-1a` at `04e60530c` (66 files); rev 3 measured T1/T2 at `5b658d681`; rev 4 re-measured all three at `b81c2fd52`. **Both active refs have moved again**, and the re-run at `c2be5a53a` is in §0 — `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `2fa724c1d` (81 files), `lane/t2-receipt-spine` `93b106461` (**86 files**, up from 18). The overlap surface is materially larger than rev 4 measured, and **T2 in particular has grown from a test fixture into an Inventory-module lane**:

| Ref | Files it adds to the overlap surface | Wave-0a item that collides |
|---|---|---|
| `lane/w-lot-a-1a` (`2fa724c1d`, 81 files) | **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**, **`apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json`**, `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` | **0a-2** (CI protected-blob registration) and **0a-1** (see below) |
| `lane/t2-receipt-spine` (`93b106461`, 86 files) | **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**, **`apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json`**, **`apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php`**, **`apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php`**, plus `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, `permissionsMap.generated.ts`, `docs/glossary.md`, `packages/shared/types/generated.d.ts` | the two above **plus 0a-9** and **0a-5's counting-item route** |
| `lane/t1-transfers-edge` (`86273346a`) | **empty diff** — T1 is an ancestor of `dev` | none |

**T2 already makes 0a-9's edit, which is the sharpest of the new collisions.** `git diff dev...lane/t2-receipt-spine -- apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php` is a one-hunk change replacing the hard-coded *"You do not have permission to view transaction destinations."* with *"You do not have permission to perform this action."* — i.e. the generic-message half of 0a-9, already done, on a branch. Landing 0a-9 in wave 0a would be two branches editing one string; landing it in 0b, after T2 merges, leaves 0a-9 with the half T2 does **not** do — adding the failing permission list to `error.details` — which is a smaller, non-conflicting change on top.

**The Inventory-routes collision is at file level, not line level, and that is still a collision under this document's own rule.** T2's hunks in `apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php` are the stock-transfer block (`@@ -97,7 +97,7 @@` and `@@ -105,13 +105,25 @@`), while 0a-5's counting-item gate is at `:295-297` — the two would not conflict textually today. But the disjointness claim this section makes is *file*-level ("every path below is absent from `git diff --name-only`"), because a hunk range is a fact about a branch's current tip and a file is a fact about its scope, and rev 4's line-level confidence is exactly what went stale twice. The counting-item gate therefore moves to 0b with the rest of the file.

**The `feature-lane-manifest.json` collision is not obvious and is the one rev 3 could not have seen.** `apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json` assigns every top-level group under `tests/Feature` to a CI lane, and for a group whose lane is **PARKED** behind `vars.SELF_HOSTED_RUNNER_READY` the `classes` count is an **enforced non-growth ceiling**, not documentation (`apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json:1`, the `note`). `tests/Feature/Identity` is exactly such a group — `"lane": "feature-lane-tenancy/Identity", "classes": 32`, parked, with the ceiling explicitly still enforced. Adding **one** class there fails `php tools/feature-lane-manifest-check.php` (a discrete step in the `backend-architecture` job, no `if:` guard) until the ceiling and the global `gated_ceiling` are raised deliberately. `lane/w-lot-a-1a` adds **seven** new `tests/Feature/Identity` classes and must therefore raise that same key; `lane/t2-receipt-spine` edits the same file for its own groups. *(Classes under `tests/Architecture` need no entry — group dispositions are scoped to `tests/Feature`, `apps/api/tools/feature-lane-manifest-check.php:205,328-334`. That is why 0a-2's ratchet test is clean and 0a-1's is not.)*

> **RULING (orchestrator, 2026-09-10 — rev 4 M-8, EXTENDED in rev 5 by gate r4 M-3, one row SUPERSEDED in rev 4.1. Wave 0a is disjoint from all three lanes, so every colliding item moves to 0b — four were identified, and THREE actually moved: 0a-1 was restored to 0a by the rev-4.1 ruling with its single-file overlap accepted.**
>
> **This table's 0a-1 row is SUPERSEDED (rev 6, gate r5 m-2).** The orchestrator's rev-4.1 ruling — recorded at 0a-1 in the task list below and in §13's editorial row — **restored 0a-1 to wave 0a** and took the owner's named override, accepting the two-integer `feature-lane-manifest.json` conflict rather than deferring the register's only P0. The row is kept verbatim rather than deleted so the reasoning that produced the deferral stays readable, but **it is not the operative disposition**; the other three rows are.
>
> | Item | Collision | Disposition |
> |---|---|---|
> | ~~**0a-1 — land S-1**~~ *(SUPERSEDED by the rev-4.1 ruling — 0a-1 stays in wave 0a)* | its own test file `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionCacheTenantScopingTest.php` (S-1's plan `:62-66`) is a **new class in the ceilinged, parked `Identity` group**, so landing it requires editing `apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json` — the same `Identity.classes` key `lane/w-lot-a-1a` must raise, in a file `lane/t2-receipt-spine` also edits | ~~moved to 0b as 0b-10~~ → **stays in 0a**; the manifest overlap is the single accepted exception |
> | **0a-2's CI registration** — the `ROUTE_COVERAGE_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB` env line in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | both `lane/w-lot-a-1a` and `lane/t2-receipt-spine` edit that file — **still true at `2fa724c1d` / `93b106461`** | **the env line moves to 0b as 0b-11**; the ratchet test, its baseline and its liveness fixture **stay in 0a**, all under `tests/Architecture/` and clean |
> | **0a-9 — the `RequireAnyPermission` message** *(rev 5)* | `lane/t2-receipt-spine` edits `apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php` and **already makes the generic-message change**, so 0a-9 and T2 would be two branches rewriting one string | **moved to 0b as 0b-12**, narrowed to the half T2 does not do: adding the failing permission list to `error.details` on top of T2's generic message |
> | **0a-5's counting-item gate** — `POST /inventory/countings/{counting}/items/{item}/count` → `can:inventory.adjust` *(rev 5)* | `lane/t2-receipt-spine` edits `apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php` (its hunks are the stock-transfer block; the counting-item route is `:295-297`, so the collision is file-level, which is the level this section's disjointness claim is made at) | **moved to 0b as 0b-13**; 0a-5 keeps Promotion ×4, Uom ×5, Menu ×3, Coupon ×3 (writes) **plus the two coupon reads** of §4.4.4 |
>
> **The cost of moving 0a-1 was stated plainly, because it is the only P0 in the register (I-1) and deferring it delays it behind a lane merge.** The tenant-scoped permission cache is the bug where one tenant can be served another tenant's permission snapshot (G11, §4.8). Moving it would mean it lands after `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merges rather than today. **The owner's override, which is the one the spec now ships:** land 0a-1 in wave 0a anyway and accept a deliberate **two-line** merge resolution on `feature-lane-manifest.json` (`Identity.classes` and `gated_ceiling`, each an integer) when the lane merges — a conflict a human resolves by addition, not by choosing a side. Rev 4 shipped the disjoint scoping, because D7 makes lane absorption the priority and because "we will just resolve the conflict" is exactly the reasoning that produced B-9; **the rev-4.1 orchestrator ruling then took the override** (§13's editorial row), so the two named outcomes were both written down and one was chosen rather than discovered.
>
> **The general rule the collision exposes, so a later 0a task does not re-create it:** *any* wave-0a item that adds a class under `apps/api/tests/Feature/` lands in a manifest-governed group and must either target a group whose lane is live (no enforced ceiling) or move to 0b. Wave 0a's remaining items add tests only under `tests/Architecture/`, which is outside the manifest's scope.

**Anti-growth is weaker in 0a until 0b-11 lands, and that is stated rather than glossed.** With the protected-blob env line deferred, `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest` enforces **growth** (a new uncovered route not in the baseline fails) and **stale** (a fixed route whose entry survives fails) from day one, but its **anti-growth** direction — the baseline key set compared against a CI-pinned blob, which is what stops a contributor adding a violation *and* its baseline entry in one commit — is inert until 0b-11 registers the variable. The two live directions are the ones that matter for the shrink schedule; anti-growth guards against a specific bad-faith pattern and waits one wave.

**Verified 0a file list (re-checked in rev 5 against all three diffs, file by file, at `2fa724c1d` / `86273346a` / `93b106461`)** — every path below was checked with `git diff --name-only dev...<ref> -- <path>` against **both** active lanes and returned empty for both:

`apps/api/tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/route-permission-coverage-baseline.json`, the ratchet's liveness fixture under `apps/api/tests/Architecture/`, `apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/AllowSelfService.php`, `apps/api/bootstrap/app.php`, `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php`, `apps/api/app/Modules/Promotion/Presentation/routes.php`, `apps/api/app/Modules/Uom/Presentation/routes.php`, `apps/api/app/Modules/Menu/Presentation/routes.php`, `apps/api/app/Modules/Coupon/Presentation/routes.php` **(coupons — rev 3 fixed rev 2's repeated `Promotion` path; the real file is `apps/api/app/Modules/Coupon/Presentation/routes.php:10-24`, minor 3)**, `docs/conventions/03-AUTHORIZATION.md`, `.claude/commands/add-permissions.md`, plus S-1's five files — `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Listeners/ScopePermissionCacheToTenant.php`, `…/RestoreCentralPermissionCache.php`, `apps/api/app/Providers/TenancyServiceProvider.php`, `apps/api/tests/Traits/ProvisionsTenantDatabases.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionCacheTenantScopingTest.php` (all five clean against both lanes; they are in 0a by the rev-4.1 ruling below).

**Removed from that list in rev 5** (gate r4, M-3): `apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php` and `apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php` — both now in `lane/t2-receipt-spine`'s 86-file diff, so 0a-9 and the counting-item gate move to 0b as 0b-12 and 0b-13. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` left the list in rev 4 and stays out. **The single remaining overlap is `apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json`**, the accepted ceiling increment named in the 0a-1 ruling below — one file, two integers, resolved by addition.

Note `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php` is **not** in either lane's diff even though `RoleController.php` and `UserController.php` are (W-LOT) — which is why 0a-4 edits the routes file and touches neither controller.

**Rev 3's prediction about T2 has come true, and rev 4's contradiction of it is withdrawn.** Rev 3 noted that *"T2 will edit `apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php`"*; rev 4 replied that the file was "still absent from T2's diff" and kept the counting-item gate in 0a. At `93b106461` that file **is** in T2's diff, along with `RequireAnyPermission.php`, `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, `permissionsMap.generated.ts` and `docs/glossary.md`. The lesson is recorded rather than the fact alone: a no-overlap claim against a moving lane has a shelf life of one measurement, which is why §0's lane table is re-run every round and why the four moved items moved rather than being re-argued.

**The rev-3 rejection of gate r2's minor 4 stands and is now moot.** Rev 3 recorded that gate r2 had T1 and T2 the wrong way round, attributing the fixture to T1. Re-run in rev 4: T1 is still empty and T2 still owns the fixture, so rev 3's correction was right; the point is now moot because T2 has grown well past one file. **Overlap check, re-verified in rev 5 against all three lanes at their current tips (§0).** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` (`2fa724c1d`, 81 files) modifies `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, `RoleController.php`, `UserController.php`, `usePermissions.ts`, `permissionsMap.generated.ts`, `generated.d.ts`, `RolesPage.tsx`, `routes/index.tsx`, `Sidebar.tsx`, the whole `BatchExpiry` module **including its `Presentation/routes.php`**, `docs/glossary.md`, the tenant migrations directory, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json` and `apps/web/playwright.config.ts`. `lane/t2-receipt-spine` (`93b106461`, **86 files**, up from 18 at rev 4) modifies `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `apps/api/tests/feature-lane-manifest.json`, **`apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php`**, **`apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php`**, `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, `permissionsMap.generated.ts`, `docs/glossary.md`, `packages/shared/types/generated.d.ts`, the Inventory Application/Domain/DTO tree, four tenant migrations and twenty files under `apps/api/tests/`. `lane/t1-transfers-edge` (`86273346a`) is **empty** against `dev`.

**Resulting wave-0a task list (rev 6 — 6 items, closing 15 uncovered writes and 6 uncovered reads):** 0a-1 land S-1, the tenant-scoped permission cache · 0a-2 route-coverage ratchet + baseline (**without** the CI env line) · 0a-3 `authz.self` middleware, alias and allow-list · 0a-4 gate the four role/permission read routes · 0a-5 gate the writes needing no new key (Promotion ×4, Uom ×5, Menu ×3, Coupon ×3) **and the two coupon reads** on `can:coupons.view` (**BatchExpiry and counting-item excluded**) · 0a-8 docs. **0a-9 has left 0a** (0b-12) alongside 0a-5's counting-item route (0b-13); **0a-1 stays** by the rev-4.1 ruling, with the manifest ceiling conflict accepted rather than avoided. ### Wave 0b — new permissions for the remaining ungated writes

**Entry condition: `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merged into local `dev`** (D7). Reason: 0b adds permission keys to `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::permissionNames()` and role grants to `rolePermissionGrants()`, which is the file that lane rewrites; landing both in parallel guarantees a conflict on an 871-line array and, worse, on the marked-tenant branch it introduces at `lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:51-54` *(rev 3 corrected rev 2's `:568`, which is the current tree's old unguarded `syncPermissions()` call; rev 5 re-derives the line at `2fa724c1d`)*. **That branch is also why 0b cannot deploy through `tenants:seed` at all — see §4.3.5 and EC-32c (B-4).**

Tasks: declare and gate `services.*` (3 keys) and `service-categories.*` (3), `channels.*` (4), `categories.*` (4), `progression.*` (2), `purchase-hub.orders.create` (1), `companies.create` (1), and **`credit-notes.cancel`** (0b-1, first). **Rev 2 adds four items relocated from wave 0a (B-9)**: **0b-5** gate the two `BatchExpiry` writes (`DELETE /api/v1/batches/{uuid}` → `batches.delete`, `POST /api/v1/batches/{uuid}/recall` → `batches.recall`) **on top of** the lane's rewritten routes file and its `BatchActionAccess` middleware, not instead of them; **0b-6** delete `PermissionSeeder.php` and its `ProductionSeeder.php:75` caller; **0b-7** fix the masking test (`apps/api/tests/Feature/Document/RefundResidualTenantIsolationTest.php:136-142`) **in the same commit as 0b-1**, so the suite is never knowingly red on a merged commit; **0b-8** the glossary rows, added on top of the lane's General-manager row. **Rev 3 adds a ninth (B-4): 0b-9** — the `permissions:ensure` command (§4.3.5), which is how every 0b key actually reaches a marked tenant, and which is deleted again in wave 1; **0b-9 additionally ships `LegacyRoleBaseline` with BOTH its predicates and its version-0 fixture** — `matchesPreWave0b()`, which `ensure` uses, and `matchesVersion0()`, which wave-1 adoption uses (§4.3.5's rev-6 B-1 ruling; they are two functions and must not collapse into one) — plus **`PermissionWriteLock`**, because `ensure` is itself a role-grant writer and must serialize against the operator edit path from the day it ships (§4.3.2's rev-6 B-2 ruling, EC-20b). Wave 1 then inherits all three rather than introducing them. **Rev 4 added two more, both relocated from wave 0a (M-8): 0b-10 — (moved back to 0a-1 by orchestrator ruling 2026-09-10)**; and **0b-11** — register `ROUTE_COVERAGE_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (one `env:` line beside the existing `DPA_BASELINE_PROTECTED_BLOB` at `:318`) plus the matching repository variable, which switches on the ratchet's anti-growth direction. **`.github/workflows/ci.yml` is edited by BOTH active lanes** (`lane/w-lot-a-1a` and `lane/t2-receipt-spine`, §8's rev-5 re-measurement), which is why 0b-11 sits behind 0b's entry condition and why its edit must be a single appended `env:` line rather than a reflow of the block — the two lanes' hunks are elsewhere in the file (T2's is the PG `--filter` allow-list), so an append merges cleanly and a reformat does not. **Rev 5 adds two more, both relocated from wave 0a by gate r4's M-3 (§8's rev-5 re-scope): 0b-12** — the `RequireAnyPermission` change, narrowed to adding the failing permission list to `error.details`, because `lane/t2-receipt-spine` already replaces the hard-coded message with a generic one (`apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php:25`); **and 0b-13** — gate `POST /inventory/countings/{counting}/items/{item}/count` on `can:inventory.adjust` (`apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php:295-297`), which uses an existing key and moves only because T2 now edits that file. **0b-5 is narrower than rev 3 described, and rev 4 says why:** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` already attaches `BatchActionAccess:batches.delete` and `:batches.recall` to the two routes (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php:27,30`), but that middleware performs **no check at all** while `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE` is false (`lane/…/Presentation/Middleware/BatchActionAccess.php:17-19`, default at `lane/…/config/lot_action_permissions.php:5`), so it is not coverage (§4.4.3). 0b-5 adds a plain `can:batches.delete` / `can:batches.recall` **beside** it. All keys land with `admin`-only template defaults except `credit-notes.cancel` (`manager`, `general_manager` — matching its siblings) and `services.*`/`service-categories.*` (`manager`, `general_manager`, because the FE alias map they replace already exposed them to managers, and removing a manager's ability to edit a service would be a functional regression, not a fix).

`POST /api/v1/companies` is the sharpest one: today `CreateCompanyRequest::authorize()` returns `true` and the controller then self-grants `MembershipRole::Owner` to the caller (`apps/api/app/Modules/Company/Presentation/Controllers/CompanyController.php:151`), so any authenticated user in any of the seven seeded roles can create a company and become its owner. It gains `can:companies.create`, `admin`-only.

Reviewers: `tenancy-authz-reviewer` + `frontend-conventions-reviewer`.

**Deploy (rewritten in rev 3 — B-4).** The T-2/T-3 sequence beginning `tenants:seed RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-transfer-destination-receipt-and-blind-receiving-design-rev-11.md:420`) **does not work after `lane/w-lot-a-1a` merges**: on a marked tenant with `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE=false` — the shipped default (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/config/lot_action_permissions.php:5`) — that seeder takes its preservation branch and writes nothing (`lane/…/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php:51-54`), so the 0b keys would never reach the tenant while its routes are already gated on them: 403 for everyone including `admin`. The deploy is therefore, in order:

1. `php artisan permissions:ensure --keys=… --grant-to=…` across every tenant, with the 0b key list and the template roles each key is declared for (§4.3.5) — additive, marker-safe, idempotent;
2. `php artisan permission:cache-reset`;
3. `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map`.

`tenants:seed RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` is **not** in this sequence at all. Verification: the `PERMISSIONS-ENSURE` marker per tenant shows `created`/`granted` on the first run and `created=0 granted=0` on a re-run; EC-32c is the test. Rollback: revert; the added `permissions` rows are inert once the routes no longer reference them, and `permissions:prune-orphans` is not run.

Overlap: this wave **owns** `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php` end to end. It cannot run concurrently with any lane that edits that file. It must be sequenced after `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s five-push staging protocol has completed, not merely after its merge, because that protocol asserts pre/post role-permission snapshots on staging boots (plan `:4169`) and a registry-driven sync running in between would invalidate the comparison.

Rollback: the migration's `down()` drops the four columns (refusing while any `template_key` is set, mirroring W-LOT-A-1a's `down()` contract at plan `:1234`); the seeder shim reverts to the array in the same revert commit; `permissions:sync` is additive, so a rollback leaves extra permission rows, which are inert.

### Wave 2 — role management, principals and tokens

**Entry condition: wave 1 merged and soaked.** Two lanes, runnable in parallel within the cap.

**Additional entry condition on 2b, from B-1 (rev 2): no scoped token may be issuable until the role-name authorization sites are converted.** `service-accounts.issue-token` stays unrouted until `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` is green — i.e. until 2a has converted `CreateDraftCountingRequest` (`:35`), the six `InventoryCountingController` bypasses (`:782,866,905,975,1297,1494`) and `DiscountPermissionResolver::isAdmin()` (`:39`) to permission checks (§4.1.3a (b)). Issuing a narrowing token while a role-name idiom can bypass the narrowing would ship the intersection guarantee as a claim rather than a property.

- **2a — role management hardening**: `roles.is_system` consumption; the single **`LastAdminFloor`** domain service wired into all nine writers of §4.6.3 (including `UserController::update`'s `syncRoles`) plus `LastAdminFloorWriterCensusTest`; F-1; D4 read gates **with the payload shaping of §4.6.4**; `RoleCreated/RoleUpdated/RoleDeleted` extending `DomainEvent` with their `identity.role.*` names, registered in `DomainEventSubscriber`; `(principal_id, token_id)` attribution merged into the **`$metadata`** argument once in `AuditService::record()` (§4.6.5's M-4 correction); denial events **on `audit_events`** with the two-key Redis dedup and its Redis integration test; **`EffectivePermissionResolver`** with `forSubject`/`forTarget`; `GET /users/{id}/effective-permissions[?token_id=]`; the effective-permissions panel; `RolesPage` grouped by registry module with the template-version banner and *Re-apply template*; **and the conversion of the eight role-name authorization sites** that gates 2b.
- **2b — principals and tokens**: `PrincipalKind`, the `users` migration (§5.1 — including making `password` nullable and the full service CHECK), the `principal_kind` census edits across every writer listed in §4.1.1, the six `service-accounts.*` permissions and their routes, **`CreateServiceAccountRequest` + `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` + `PUT /service-accounts/{id}/memberships` (§4.1.2)**, the seat exclusion **across all six counters** plus the `max_service_accounts` constant, its per-tier defaults and `canAddServiceAccount()` (§4.1.7), **`EnforceTokenScope` attached to the global `api` group with `EnforceTokenScopeCoverageTest` and `EnforceTokenScopeNoOpTest` (§4.1.4)**, the generalised `tokenScopePermissionNames()`, the `tenant:` claim at issuance, `TokenScopeData` on `/auth/me`, the `X-Company-Id` contract, **`MembershipRevocationService`** and the queued `RevokePrincipalTokens` retry, and the FE service-account + token screens.

2b depends on 2a only for the audit-event plumbing (`actorTokenId`), so 2a merges first. The FE deletions of §4.5.4 (`uiAliasPermissions.ts`, the fallback) ride with **2a**, because they depend on wave 0b having made `services.*` real, not on tokens.

Reviewers: `tenancy-authz-reviewer` (both, mandatory) + `frontend-conventions-reviewer` (both). Deploy: `tenants:migrate-rolling`, `permissions:sync`, `permission:cache-reset`, `permissions:export-frontend-map`.

**Rollback — `down()` restores the PRIOR schema, which is four operations, not one (rev 3, M-7).** Rev 2 said only "the three `users` columns are dropped after service tokens are revoked", which leaves service rows with `password IS NULL` and `email IS NULL` in a schema that no longer identifies them as services — and makes restoring `password NOT NULL` impossible while they remain. The migration's `down()` is therefore, in order:

1. **Refuse** while any `personal_access_tokens` row belongs to a `principal_kind = 'service'` principal — the operator revokes them first (the `DELETE /service-accounts/{id}/tokens/{tokenId}` surface, or `permissions:…`-independent `sanctum:prune-expired`); this is rev 2's condition, kept.
2. **Delete every `principal_kind = 'service'` `users` row** and its `user_company_memberships` and `model_has_roles` rows. **This is data loss and is stated as such**: a rollback of wave 2b destroys every service account the tenant created, because there is no representation for one in the pre-wave schema — a machine identity with no password and no email is not a user the old `users` table can hold. The `down()` logs each deleted principal id and writes an `AuditEvent` per deletion before removing it, so the rollback is reconstructible from the audit chain even though the rows are gone. An operator who wants them back re-runs `up()` and re-creates them; the tokens were already revoked at step 1, so nothing continues to authenticate.
3. **Drop the CHECK**, then drop `principal_kind`, `principal_description`, `created_by_user_id`.
4. **Restore `password NOT NULL`** — safe only because step 2 removed every row that could hold a null. Without step 2 this statement fails and the whole `down()` aborts, which is the loud failure the ordering exists to avoid.

The `roles` migration's `down()` (wave 1) keeps its rev-2 contract and gains the same explicitness: it drops the two partial uniques and the three CHECKs, then the four columns, and **refuses while any row has `template_key IS NOT NULL`** (mirroring W-LOT-A-1a's `down()` contract at plan `:1234`) — so a rollback after a sync is a deliberate act that first requires clearing adoption, and never silently discards which roles were system roles.

### Wave 3 — consistency sweep

**Entry condition: wave 2 merged.** One enforcement style (move the 130 in-controller checks and the 46 FormRequest-only gates to route middleware, deleting baseline entries as it goes); empty the PHPStan ignore list; retire `LotActionPermissionDelta` **as dead code** — sync never called it (§4.3.0a), so retirement is a deletion once every tenant carries the marker, with no re-pointing and no behavioural change — and reconcile `SystemRoleName` with `SystemRoleTemplate`; delete the `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` static shims and point the exporter at the registry; POS legacy ladder removal and dead-`pos.*` pruning; `MembershipRole` reconciliation (remove or document `UserController.php:929` `isOwner()` as the sole authz read); `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm`; the `marketplace.admin` re-scoping ticket; the per-role regression matrix test (a table of role × action → 200/403, exercising **deny** paths, which today's `RBACTest` never does).

### Wave 4 — edge-case campaign

**Entry condition: wave 3 merged.** The §7 register driven as a journey in the real UI on a fresh tenant with a second company and a second location, with 5xx and console capture on every probe (convention 09 and the 2026-08-31 empirical-evidence rule), feeding `docs/qa/MANUAL-TESTING-LOOP.md`. A green run plus a clean `tenant:census-day-one` is the promotion precondition for the whole programme.

### Sequencing summary

```
now ──► 0a ──┬────────────────────────────────────────────────► (independent)
             │
lane/w-lot-a-1a merged ──► 0b ──► 1 ──► 2a ──► 2b ──► 3 ──► 4
                                   ▲
        lane/w-lot-a-1a staging protocol complete ┘
```

`lane/t1-transfers-edge` and `lane/t2-receipt-spine` are independent of every wave except that T-2/T-3 introduces `inventory.transfers.reconcile` and `inventory.transfers.close` into the seeder (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-transfer-destination-receipt-and-blind-receiving-design-rev-11.md:398-420`). If T-2/T-3 merges **before** wave 1, the two keys are simply declared in `InventoryPermissionManifest` like any other; if **after**, wave 1's manifest already contains them and T-2/T-3's seeder edit becomes a manifest edit. Either order works; the manifest is the only thing that changes.

## 9. Owner rulings applied, and what is still open

### 9.1 Rulings applied (verbatim from the decision sheet, with the benchmark cell that supported each)

Source: `docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/06-synthesis.md:84-93`. Each row is the owner's accepted default, quoted, with the section that applies it. **None is reopened by this spec.**

| ID | Question (verbatim) | Benchmark cell (verbatim) | Ruling applied | Applied in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **D1** | *"Roles scoped per tenant (current) or per company?"* | Odoo: *"Groups are database-global; multi-company by record rules"*; ERPNext: *"Roles global; User Permission restricts Company"*; Dolibarr: *"Groups per entity in multicompany module"* | *"**Keep tenant-scoped**; company/location stays on membership"* | §2 (Role, Membership rows), §4.6.3 clause b, §5.2, §6 |
| **D2** | *"What happens to the 7 seeded roles when a deploy adds a permission?"* | Odoo: *"Module upgrade re-applies group definitions unless `noupdate`"*; ERPNext: *"`bench migrate` re-syncs standard DocPerms; custom perms kept separately"*; Dolibarr: *"Module re-activation re-inserts rights"* | *"**Template-delta**: seeded roles track a template version; additive deltas applied on sync unless the tenant has customised that role (flag set on first tenant edit) — then admin-only + notice in the Roles UI"* | §4.3.2 steps 4–6, §4.6.6, §5.2, EC-9/EC-10 |
| **D3** | *"Ungated routes: deny-by-default ratchet from a shrinking baseline, or hard fail now?"* | Odoo: *"ORM denies without ACL row"*; ERPNext: *"`@frappe.whitelist` without `has_permission` caused CVEs"*; Dolibarr: *"Rights checked per page"* | *"**Ratchet with ceilinged baseline** (repo pattern), wave 0 gates the 53 writes first"* | §4.4.3 (two separate ceilings so reads never buy write headroom), §4.4.4, wave 0a/0b |
| **D4** | *"Role read endpoints: open, or `roles.view`?"* | Odoo: *"Access rights visible to admins only"*; ERPNext: *"Role Permission Manager = System Manager"*; Dolibarr: *"Admin only"* | *"**`roles.view`** for the matrix; role *names* remain readable to `users.assign-roles` holders"* | §4.6.4 — the any-of gate on the list plus a **response shaped by the gate**, which is what makes "names but not permissions" real rather than nominal |
| **D5** | *"Naming convention and outlier migration (`catalog_cart`, `pos_orders`, `uom.edit`, `deliveries.edit`)?"* | Odoo: *"`module.group_x`"*; ERPNext: *"`DocType + verb`"*; Dolibarr: *"`module->object->action`"* | *"**Freeze `kebab-resource.verb` + verb enum**; rename outliers via sync rename map"* | §4.2.2, §4.9.2 — and the deliberate DIVERGE from the audit's own G13 suggestion of Filament Shield's `{verb}_{resource}`, recorded in the §1 G13 cell |
| **D6** | *"Generalise per-user permission overrides beyond discount flags?"* | Odoo: *"Not supported (group-only)"*; ERPNext: *"User Permissions (row-scope only)"*; Dolibarr: *"Per-user rights grid"* | *"**No** for now; keep overrides to discount numerics; revisit after launch"* | §3.2, §4.6.6 (surfaced read-only in the effective-permissions view, never granted) |
| **D7** | *"Sequencing against in-flight W-LOT-A-1a (general_manager, batch permissions) and T-1..T-3 (`inventory.transfers.reconcile`)"* | — (no benchmark cell; a sequencing question) | *"Wave 0 lands **after** W-LOT-A-1a merges; the registry migration absorbs both lanes' permissions"* | §4.3.0, §4.3.0a (the superset table and the rev-4 retirement of the sync↔delta coupling), §4.9.1, §8. Refined with evidence, and **re-scoped in rev 2**: wave 0a needs no dependency **only after four items moved to 0b** — rev 1's 0a claimed `apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php` and `docs/glossary.md`, both of which that lane rewrites (B-9). With those moved, the verified 0a file list is disjoint from `git diff --name-only dev...lane/w-lot-a-1a`; 0b onward waits for the merge, and wave 1 additionally waits for that lane's staging protocol, not merely its merge |
| **D8** | *"Role mutation audit + denial logging into the audit chain (not the fiscal chain)?"* | Odoo: *"Opt-in tracking"*; ERPNext: *"Version tracking mandatory on Role"*; Dolibarr: *"Security events log"* | *"**Yes**, audit events for role create/update/delete and for 403 denials on write routes"* | §4.6.5 — with two refinements the ruling did not specify and a gate will ask about: the mutation event carries the **diff**, not the resulting set; and denials are deduplicated per `(principal, permission, route)` per 5 minutes with a `suppressed_count`, because unbounded denial logging is how denial logging gets turned off |

**And the new requirement (owner, 2026-09-10):** *"later we might use the MCP through service accounts, so every user that has an MCP should also have their permissions reflected."* Applied in §4.1 in full: service accounts are principals in the `users` table with ordinary memberships and roles (§4.1.1); their MCP/API tokens narrow, never widen (§4.1.3); the enforcement point is one middleware plus the existing `User::hasPermissionTo()` intersection, so **all 917 counted permission-based `Gate`/`can` sites** — 608 route middleware strings, 202 `->can()` calls and 107 `Gate::authorize()` calls (`docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/01-backend-permission-model.md:78-90`) — inherit it without an edit (§4.1.4), **while the eight role-name sites do not and their conversion stays an entry condition of token issuance** (§4.1.3a (b), wave 2a, `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest`). *(Rev 6, gate r5 m-6: rev 5 said "all 917 existing authorization call sites", which reads as a claim about every authorization site and is contradicted by §4.1.3a's own table and by wave 2a's prerequisite. The count is right; the noun was too wide.)* attribution is `(principal_id, token_id)` on every audit row (§4.1.5); and the MCP's discovery call is the **unchanged** `/auth/me` plus one additive `token_scope` field (§4.1.6). G16 (§1) records that this is a deliberate DIVERGE from all three reference ERPs, none of which can scope a token below its user, and a MATCH with the industry norm they depart from.

### 9.2 Open questions (four; each with a benchmark and a recommended default, and the design is written against that default)

**Rev 2 changes to this section.** OQ-1's honesty is fixed (§§1–8 *do* hard-code its recommended answer, and now say so). OQ-2 is stated against the real current behaviour. **The old OQ-3 is deleted** — it reopened settled D8 and proposed a table that §5 said did not exist; denial events go on `audit_events` (§4.6.5, B-8). Its number is reused by a genuinely open question the gate surfaced: **the SoD baseline retirement**. OQ-4 stands, now that B-5 has made it safe to ask.

**OQ-1 — Do service accounts count toward the last-admin floor?**
*Benchmark.* No reference ERP has the concept, so none rules. The adjacent norm is Frappe Cloud's, which withholds the `Administrator` credential from tenant customers entirely and makes `System Manager` the human ceiling — i.e. the credential that can always recover a tenant is deliberately held by a *person*, not a process ([administrator](https://docs.frappe.io/erpnext/v13/user/manual/en/setting-up/users-and-permissions/administrator)). GitHub's equivalent rule is that an organisation must retain at least one human owner; a bot account cannot be the sole owner.
*Recommended default (applied throughout §4.6.3, EC-4):* **No.** A tenant whose only administrator is a machine has nobody who can log in when the token is lost, revoked or expired. The cost of the default is that a fully-automated tenant must keep one human admin, which is the correct answer to "who do we call".

*Rev 2 — what a "yes" would cost, stated honestly.* §0 claimed nothing in §§1–8 depends on an unresolved answer. That is not true of OQ-1: the recommended answer is **hard-coded** in the F-2 definition (§4.6.3), in EC-4 and EC-4a, and in the §2 Service-account row. If the owner rules **yes** (a service admin satisfies the floor), the edits are: drop `principal_kind = human` from *A*; drop EC-4; and re-word the Service-account glossary row. That is a small, bounded diff — but it is a diff, and rev 1 should have said so rather than claiming independence.

**OQ-2 — What is the default TTL for a service-account token?**
*Rev 2 — the current AutoERP behaviour, which rev 1 got wrong.* This is **not** a greenfield policy question: the repository already sets a global 30-day TTL (`'expiration' => env('SANCTUM_TOKEN_EXPIRATION', 43200)`, `apps/api/config/sanctum.php:43-53`), and a custom validation callback makes a per-row `expires_at` **override** that global (`apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php:201-219`). So today web back-office tokens expire in 30 days by the global and POS terminal tokens live one year by an explicit `expires_at` (`apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/AuthController.php:146-176,294-310`). Any answer here **changes** that policy and must say what happens to the two existing token populations.

*Benchmark.* Sanctum upstream: none by default — *"By default, Sanctum tokens never expire"* — with a global `expiration` minutes setting, a per-token `expiresAt`, and `sanctum:prune-expired` ([§Token Expiration](https://laravel.com/docs/12.x/sanctum#token-expiration)). Odoo 18 moved the other way and made expiry **mandatory for non-admins**, with a per-group `api_key_duration` ceiling ([res_users.py#L195,2350,2413](https://github.com/odoo/odoo/blob/18.0/odoo/addons/base/models/res_users.py#L2350)). ERPNext and Dolibarr have no expiry at all. GitHub fine-grained PATs default to a bounded lifetime and cap custom values.
*Recommended default:* **365 days for a service token, set explicitly at issue with no unlimited option**, plus `sanctum:prune-expired --hours=24` on the daily schedule. Odoo 18 is the only reference system that revisited this recently and it moved toward mandatory expiry; a year is long enough that rotation is an annual chore rather than a weekly outage, and short enough that an abandoned integration's credential dies. **Human back-office tokens would LENGTHEN from today's 30 days to 90 days** — a lengthening, which is what makes this a real question rather than a formality *(rev 4, minor 6: rev 3 wrote "shorten" and then called it a lengthening in the same sentence; 30 → 90 days is longer)* — and **POS terminal tokens keep their explicit one-year `expires_at` unchanged**, because shortening them is an offline-till outage. All three are expressed as explicit per-token `expires_at` values rather than by moving the global, so the override semantics already in `AppServiceProvider` keep working and no existing token's lifetime changes retroactively.

**OQ-3 (replacement) — Should any of the eleven baselined SoD combinations be retired?**
*Context (recomputed in rev 3 — the question rev 2 put was against an incomplete table).* SoD-1 (§4.7) is a hard rule for new resources and a shrink-only ratchet over **eleven** combinations the shipped templates already carry, across two roles rather than one: `manager` violates seven of the eight declared groups — `invoices.create` + `.post` (`:598`), `credit-notes.create` + `.post` (`:599`), `purchase-orders.create` + `.confirm` (`:594`), `supplier-invoices.create-pending` + `.approve-invoice-first` (`:595`), `payments.create` + `.pay-supplier`/`.allocate`/`.refund` (`:609`), `expenses.create` + `.post`/`.pay` (`:605`), `inventory.adjustments.create` + `.post` (`:603`) — and **`accountant`** violates four: `supplier-invoice` (`:829`), `expense` (`:832`), `payment` (`:836`) and `journal` — `journal.create` + `journal.post` (`:841`), all in `apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`. The count becomes **eighteen** after `lane/w-lot-a-1a`, because `general_manager` inherits `manager`'s set (lane seeder `:97-98`). Removing any of them narrows what a real manager or accountant can do on day one.
*Benchmark.* **Odoo** ships create and approve as distinct groups on purchases (the Purchase Order Double Approval group) but leaves a single-user small deployment free to hold both — the separation is a *shipped default on one flow*, not a runtime rule ([approvals](https://odoo-users.readthedocs.io/en/latest/purchase/purchases/rfq/approvals.html)). **ERPNext** gates each workflow transition per role, so separation is expressed per document type and is opt-in through Workflow ([workflow](https://docs.frappe.io/erpnext/workflow)). **Dolibarr** states it as convention only: do not grant validate to the group that has create. **None of the three implements NIST INCITS 359 SSD/DSD generically**, and none forbids the combination in a shipped default across the board. The domain norm is therefore: separate create from approve **where the flow is financially decisive and the tenant is large enough to staff it**, and let small tenants collapse the roles.
*Recommended default:* **retire three from `manager`, keep the rest, and change nothing on `accountant`.** The three are `invoices.create` + `.post`, `credit-notes.create` + `.post` and `payments.create` + `.refund` — the ones where the benchmark's own shipped defaults separate (a document becoming a fiscal fact, and money leaving) and where a two-person tenant can still operate by granting the second half to `general_manager`, which exists precisely to be the second pair of eyes. The rest stay with `manager` (`purchase-orders.confirm`, `supplier-invoices.approve-invoice-first`, `expenses.post`/`.pay`, `inventory.adjustments.post`), because a five-person pharmacy legitimately has one person who raises and confirms a PO — and forcing an admin into that loop is how operators end up sharing the admin login, which is a worse outcome than the SoD gap. **`accountant`'s four are recommended untouched**: an accountant who cannot post the journal they wrote is not an accountant, and in every reference system the bookkeeping role holds both halves of the journal by design; separation there is a four-eyes review at period close, not a permission split. **This is an owner decision and the design ships the baseline unchanged until it is made**; the ratchet guarantees the number can only fall. Applying the recommendation would take `SOD_BASELINE_CEILING` from 18 to 15 (three retired from `manager`, and the same three from `general_manager`, which mirrors it — so **12**, if the owner rules the mirror follows, which is the second half of the question).

**OQ-4 — After wave 1, does `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT` default to `true` in production?**
*Benchmark.* Both reference systems that solved this sync it automatically: Odoo re-applies `ir.model.access.csv` on every install **and** upgrade, and ERPNext syncs DocPerms on every `bench migrate` — neither asks an operator to opt in, because a permission that exists in code and not in the database is a broken feature ([security tutorial](https://www.odoo.com/documentation/19.0/developer/tutorials/server_framework_101/04_securityintro.html), [bench migrate](https://docs.frappe.io/framework/user/en/bench/reference/migrate)). Dolibarr is the outlier that requires a human to re-activate the module, and is the one with the worst drift story.
*Preconditions (rev 3 added two the gate named; rev 4 restated one; rev 5 adds the one that makes the question answerable at all).* The question is safe to ask only once **four** things hold, and all four are now specified: **B-5** — the fleet runner exits non-zero on any blocked or failed tenant; **B-8** — the status file is written on both arms and read by `HealthCheckService`, so a recovered boot clears the state and a failed one is visible on the admin health endpoint; **r3 B-1/B-2** — the sync is idempotent and touches no role before adoption has classified it, which is only true once the W-LOT delta invocation is retired (§4.3.0a); and — **new in rev 5, gate r4 B-3** — **the flag is the only deploy-time trigger there is.** Rev 4 also specified an unconditional `DatabaseMigrated` listener, and `tenants:migrate-rolling` runs unconditionally before the flag block, so the flag governed nothing: `false` still synced every rolling-migrated tenant and `true` synced each one twice. Asking the owner to rule on a flag that does not control the behaviour it names is not a question, it is a formality. With the listener removed (§4.3.3's B-3 ruling), `SYNC_PERMISSIONS_ON_BOOT` is the complete answer to "does a deploy sync". Rev 3 named the idempotency precondition as "the sync's inputs are defined and frozen"; the frozen inputs were themselves the defect, and the precondition is properly stated as *a second boot sync writes nothing* — **EC-36** is its test. **B-4** is a wave-0b concern rather than a precondition here: `permissions:ensure` is deleted before wave 1 ships, so the flag never governs it. Without those four, a partial fleet sync would have been indistinguishable from a clean one (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:148-170` continues past errors); with them, "on by default" becomes a decision about convenience rather than about visibility.
*Recommended default:* **Yes — `true` in production, after the one-week staging soak in §8 shows zero unintended diffs.** The reason the flag exists today is that the thing it ran was destructive (I-20); once it runs an additive, template-delta sync that provably never touches a custom role or a customised system role, keeping it off would reproduce the original problem — new permissions silently missing on live tenants, which is exactly the recurring "→ 403" gap the entrypoint comment already records for `uom.view` (2026-06) and `loyalty.enroll` (2026-07) (`apps/api/docker/entrypoint.sh:156-161`). The conservative alternative — leave it `false` and run `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet` as a named deploy step (never `tenants:run`, per B-5) — is a real option and costs one runbook line; it is worse only because a step a human can forget is a step a human will forget.

---

## 10. Out of scope

Each item below is deliberately excluded and stays on its own ticket; none is a prerequisite for any wave.

| Item | Why out, where it lives |
|---|---|
| **Super-admin MFA** and the **TN impersonation legal gate** (E-4) | Platform-operator hardening, not tenant RBAC. `docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/06-synthesis.md:105` |
| **Discount policy redesign** and **cashier price override** | Deferred by the owner 2026-07-09; D6 keeps the numerics as they are, §4.6.6 only displays them |
| **Country-code mutability** | Unrelated ticket |
| **Replacing Spatie with a policy/ABAC engine** | Direction C, rejected — the benchmark shows all three reference ERPs succeed with role-based actions plus a separate scope layer, which is what exists |
| **Per-company roles** | D1 ruled against; would be a second surface for one concept (convention 11) |
| **General per-user permission overrides** | D6 ruled against |
| **A permission-creation UI** | Permissions are code (§2, Permission row). A tenant-authored permission would have no call site and no label, and would break the registry's role as the boundary of what `admin` holds |
| **OAuth2 / client-credentials flow** | Sanctum PATs cover the MCP and integration cases; an authorization-code flow is a separate lane if a third-party marketplace ever needs one |
| **Deleting orphan permission rows automatically** | §4.3.2 step 3 reports, never deletes; `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` is wave 3 and is a deliberate operator action |
| **`marketplace.admin` platform re-scoping** | Wave 3 + ticket `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-marketplace-admin-platform-scope.md`; unreachable today behind the module kill-switch (`apps/api/app/Modules/Marketplace/Presentation/routes.php:49`) |
| **`RoleController` `CrossTenantRoute` annotation corrections** | Owned by `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s own ticket `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-09-role-controller-team-scope-annotations.md` (plan `:1420`); this design does not touch those annotations |
| **Closing the 5-minute `/auth/me` staleness window** | EC-17 — a UX exposure, not a security one; costs a websocket or a poll, and every write is still refused server-side |
| **Location scope on batch writes** | `DELETE /batches/{uuid}` and `POST /batches/{uuid}/recall` resolve and mutate a batch without consulting `LocationScopeResolver` (`apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/Controllers/BatchController.php:176-188,193-210`; the resolver is used only by the expiry read at `:252`), and the lane's `BatchActionAccess` checks permission activation only (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:…/Middleware/BatchActionAccess.php:16-26`). 0b-5 gates them on **permission**, which is this design's scope; adding **location** scope to a batch write is an inventory-behaviour change with a stock-ownership question behind it, so it is wave 3 + ticket `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-batch-writes-ignore-location-scope.md` (rev 5, gate r4 M-2) |

## 11. Change log rev 1 → rev 2 (Codex spec gate r1 fix round) — condensed

Register: `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-spec-codex-gate-r1.md` (9 BLOCKER / 9 MAJOR / 5 minor / 1 citation audit, CHANGES-REQUIRED). **All 9 BLOCKERs, all 9 MAJORs and all 5 minors were closed in rev 2**, each re-verified against the tree before being applied; the per-finding table with its `verified at` lines and anchors is preserved verbatim in that register alongside rev 2's own dispositions, and this section is condensed in rev 3 so §12 is the register a gate reads. What rev 2 changed, one line each: **B-1** the §4.1.3a idiom table, `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` + ESLint companion with an exact baseline, conversion made an entry condition of token issuance, `EffectivePermissionResolver::forSubject`/`forTarget`. **B-2** the delta adopted as template migration #1 with `LegacyRoleBaseline` adoption and the `customised_at` writer moved into wave 1. **B-3** the General-manager glossary row adopted verbatim and EC-15 reversed to a 422 refusal. **B-4** the single `LastAdminFloor` service, nine-writer table, membership clause and deterministic lock order. **B-5** `permissions:sync-fleet` replacing `tenants:run`, `--force` removed. **B-6** `PermissionVerb::Close`, the `manage`/CRUD invariant with its `settings` grandfather, AI-1 restated with `permissions:scaffold`. **B-7** the `authz.self` exact allow-list plus structural check, `TOMBSTONE` classification. **B-8** denials on `audit_events`, no `security_events` table, old OQ-3 deleted. **B-9** wave 0a re-scoped, four items moved to 0b. **M-1** the principal invariants, nullable `password`, null `email`, kind check before `Hash::check`, seat ruling. **M-2** abilities are TEXT, the real 30-day TTL, `MembershipRevocationService`, EC-22 withdrawn. **M-3** D4 payload shaping on `userRoles`. **M-4** events extending `DomainEvent` with `identity.role.*` names. **M-5** the unknown-literal error class and `RoutePermissionLiteralTest`. **M-6** the convention-09/10/11 corrections. **M-7** thirteen edge-case rows rewritten, seven added. **M-8** `DB::afterCommit`. **M-9** the single authoritative 22-key deprecation set.

**One citation-audit row was partially rejected in rev 2 and the rejection stands:** the register called *"email remains unique using original users migration `:34`"* stale. The substance was accepted (§5.1 cites the partial unique at `2026_03_23_000001_make_user_email_nullable.php:14-23`), but the line number was correct for the constraint it named — `apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2025_11_30_000003_create_users_table.php:34` is `$table->unique(['tenant_id', 'email']);`. Gate r2 re-checked this and **agreed** (`…-gate-r2.md:38`, "REJECTED-correctly").

**Rejected — one row, with evidence.** The register's **minor 4** and its matching citation-audit row state that *"`lane/t1-transfers-edge` is no longer empty … it contains `apps/api/tests/Fixtures/Inventory/transfer-reader-baseline.json:1`. That fixture does not overlap wave 0a … `lane/t2-receipt-spine` remains empty."* Re-run at `5b658d681`, both commands disagree with that assignment: `git diff --stat dev...lane/t1-transfers-edge` (`86273346a`) is **empty**, and `git diff --stat dev...lane/t2-receipt-spine` (`b41183f50`) is the **one file, 206 insertions**. The gate's *substance* is accepted and applied — rev 2's "empty for both" was stale and both lanes are now re-measured and stated individually — but the **branch identity is reversed**, and the no-overlap conclusion holds for either reading because wave 0a's verified file list contains no test fixture. Recorded so a later gate does not restore the reversal.

**Nothing else was rejected.** All eight BLOCKERs, all nine MAJORs, all eight minors and every other citation row were reproduced against the tree before being applied. The register's own "Rejected false positives" and "Preserve" lists were honoured in full: the **326 / 177 / 149 → 167 / 148** ratchet arithmetic (independently recomputed above and unchanged), the live-router feasibility of the ratchet and of the `authz.self` allow-list, Direction B, D1–D8, the `forSubject`/`forTarget` split, the unnarrowed POS PIN payload and the exclusion of service principals from PIN populations, the W-LOT marker and exact general-manager grant set, the equality-based adoption ruling, the single `LastAdminFloor` with its `users.id`-ascending lock order, the fleet runner's per-tenant outcomes and absent `--force`, `admin = activeKeys()`, central Sanctum storage with per-row expiry override and no central migration, and the generated TS union under rule 7 — none is reopened or weakened by rev 3.

## 13. Change log rev 3 → rev 4 (Codex spec gate r3 fix round) — superseded as the gate's register by §14

*Read §14 first: it is the register for the current revision.*

Register: `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-spec-codex-gate-r3.md` (4 BLOCKER / 9 MAJOR / 8 minor / 1 citation audit, verdict CHANGES-REQUIRED), read at `13947608c`; prompt at `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-spec-gate-RBAC-round-3-2026-09-10.md`. Every finding below was **re-verified against the tree at `b81c2fd52`** before being applied, and every lane citation was **re-read at `lane/w-lot-a-1a@f2d79c9a8`** — the lane has moved since rev 3's `04e60530c` and the three lane diffs were re-measured (§0). Orchestrator rulings are marked **[R]** and are binding. §§11–13 are the earlier rounds; **§14 is the register a gate reads for rev 5.**

**The headline of this round is a removal, not an addition.** Rev 2 made `permissions:sync` invoke `LotActionPermissionDelta`; rev 3 wrapped that invocation in `TemplateMigration001WLotA1a` to make it callable with frozen inputs. Gate r3 showed the wrapper could not satisfy its own two obligations, and the orchestrator's ruling **retires the machinery rather than patching it**: the delta runs once at its own lane's deploy, `template_version = 0` is the post-A-1a state, adoption precedes every grant write, and sync creates a missing `general_manager` itself — writing the marker on exactly that path. `TemplateMigration`, `TemplateMigration001WLotA1a`, the container tag `permission.template-migrations`, the two `template_migrations_*` counters, `TemplateMigrationFrozenInputTest` and `PermissionSyncService::syncRole()` have **no operational design reference left in this document** *(rev 5, gate r4 m-4: rev 4 said "all gone", which is false — the names survive in §4.3.0a's explanation of why they were retired, in this change log and in §12, all of them historical prose; what is gone is every specification of behaviour that uses them)*. The step count falls from ten to nine and §4.3.0a is shorter than it was.

**Nothing was rejected in this round.** All four BLOCKERs, all nine MAJORs and all eight minors were reproduced against the tree at `b81c2fd52` (and against `lane/w-lot-a-1a@f2d79c9a8`) before being applied. Two findings were applied in a **narrower** form than the register stated, and both narrowings are strengthenings rather than partial acceptances, recorded here so a gate can challenge them:

1. **M-3's six Treasury/Expense senders are one fix, not six.** The register listed `GenerateRecurringExpensesCommand`, three `ReconcileTreasuryCommand` sites and two `InstrumentMaturityAlertsCommand` sites as separate omissions. Every one of them calls `TreasuryAlertRecipients::forCompany()` (`apps/api/app/Modules/Treasury/Application/Services/TreasuryAlertRecipients.php:23-41`) and none builds its own recipient query, so the human predicate belongs on that service's builder at `:34-38`. Applying it six times would have produced five redundant edits and overstated the blast radius. The **selector** count, which is what the census test keys on, rises from three to five.
2. **M-2's endpoint list is stated from the controller, not from the register's names.** The register named "store/update/destroy/deactivate/invite/reset". There is no `invite` method: the invitation is a branch inside `store()` (`UserController.php:281-292`), and `activate()` (`:509`) — which the register did name in its body but not in its heading — is in the list. §4.1.2's table is derived from the controller's own public methods so it cannot drift from the file.

**One correction rev 4 found on its own, outside the register.** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` **already** attaches `BatchActionAccess:batches.delete` and `:batches.recall` to the two routes wave 0b-5 was to gate (`lane/w-lot-a-1a:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php:27,30`) — but that middleware returns `$next($request)` with **no permission check at all** while `LotActionPermissionActivation::enforced()` is false (`lane/…/Presentation/Middleware/BatchActionAccess.php:17-19`), which is the shipped default (`lane/…/config/lot_action_permissions.php:5`). A flag-conditional middleware is therefore **not** coverage, the ratchet's gating list stays exactly the five aliases, and `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest::a_flag_conditional_middleware_is_not_gating` pins `BatchActionAccess` as the worked example (§4.4.3). This is what 0b-5's "on top of, not instead of" means concretely, and without it two live writes would have been marked gated on every tenant that never flipped the flag.

**The register's "Preserve" list was honoured in full.** Direction B and D1–D8; tenant-scoped roles and D2's customised-role protection; the write-first ratchet with `326 / 177 / 149 → 167 / 148` and the sixteen wave-0a write closures (unchanged — moving S-1 closes no route); `roles.view`, kebab-resource keys, no per-user overrides, sequencing after W-LOT-A-1a, audit-chain events; first-class human/service principals and `owner grants ∩ token scope`; the `forSubject()`/`forTarget()` split and the intentionally unnarrowed POS PIN payload; global `api` attachment and priority ordering for `EnforceTokenScope`, with the central-subject no-op added **and no return to a route allow-list**; central `personal_access_tokens`, nullable TEXT abilities and per-row expiry precedence; W-LOT's exact `general_manager` grant set, its glossary row, its sole assignment surface, its public marker repository and the NULL-team no-re-homing ruling; `admin = PermissionRegistry::activeKeys()`; the exact `authz.self` allow-list and separate tombstone classification; the human-only last-admin floor with deterministic locking and all nine mutation paths; `roles.view` payload shaping and self-only effective-permission access; immutable versioned `identity.role.*` and `authz.denied` events on `audit_events`; tenant-only additive migrations, the two topology-aware partial role indexes, nullable service password/email and the service-row CHECK; the eleven-entry SoD baseline rising to eighteen and the eight SoD groups; and the generated TypeScript union under rule 7.

**The one item the register asked to preserve that rev 4 deliberately changes, with the ruling that authorises it.** The register's Preserve list says: *"The settled full-sync order create → template migrations → adopt → template deltas → admin → after-commit cache. Repairs must make those phases safe rather than silently dropping the ruling."* Rev 4 **drops the template-migration phase entirely**, on the orchestrator's explicit ruling (§4.3.0a), because the gate's own B-1 and B-2 show the phase cannot be made safe *in that position* — ahead of adoption it violates D2, and behind adoption it still re-creates rename sources. The ruling is not a silent drop: the phase's two jobs are re-homed and named. *Provisioning `general_manager`* becomes step 5, which writes the marker on exactly that path. *Bringing a tenant to the lane's end state* becomes the definition of `template_version = 0`, so the state is the baseline rather than something applied to reach it. The remaining order — **create → adopt → create roles → deltas → admin → after-commit cache** — preserves every safety property the settled order was reaching for, and adds one the settled order could not have: **no role is written before it has been classified.**

Register: `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-spec-codex-gate-r4.md` (4 BLOCKER / 4 MAJOR / 4 minor / 1 citation audit, verdict CHANGES-REQUIRED), read at `43ac8b068`; prompt at `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-spec-gate-RBAC-round-4-2026-09-10.md`. Orchestrator rulings are marked **[R]** and are binding.

**The headline of this round is that three of the four blockers are the same failure in different places: a rule stated in one section and contradicted by the mechanism in another.** Adoption compared a canonical-in-principle grant set against a snapshot that predated two mandatory transformations; `TemplateDeltaApplier` was described as a diff and implemented as an add-plus-sweep; and the boot flag was declared the sync trigger beside a listener that triggered sync unconditionally. Each is closed by making the mechanism match the rule rather than by softening the rule.

No such route exists — the alias is registered at `apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:118` and attached nowhere in the tree — so the test targets the route whose location denial is genuinely enforced today, by the `ValidLocationAccess` **rule** inside `StoreStockAdjustmentRequest` behind a real `can:` gate, and the middleware's zero attachments are stated as a fact a later lane may want to act on. ## 15. Change log rev 5 → rev 6 (Codex spec gate r5 fix round)

Register: `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-spec-codex-gate-r5.md` (2 BLOCKER / 3 MAJOR / 7 minor / 1 citation audit, verdict CHANGES-REQUIRED), read at `3107eeba7`; prompt at `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-spec-gate-RBAC-round-5-2026-09-10.md`. Every finding below was **re-verified against the tree at `02862c91d`** before being applied, and every lane reference was re-checked: `lane/w-lot-a-1a` `2fa724c1d`, `lane/t1-transfers-edge` `86273346a` and `lane/t2-receipt-spine` `93b106461` are **unchanged since rev 5**, so §8 needed no re-scope for the first round since rev 2. Orchestrator rulings are marked **[R]** and are binding.

**The headline of this round is that both blockers are the cost of a rule that was right and a mechanism that did not carry it across a boundary.** Rev 5 settled the v0 oracle correctly for *adoption* and then reused it one wave earlier, where it was unsatisfiable by construction; and it settled `customised_at` as the D2 guard and then serialized only sync against sync, leaving the guard's own writer outside the lock. Both are closed by naming the second thing — a second predicate, and a second class of lock-taker — rather than by weakening the settled rule.

**Nothing was rejected in this round.** Both BLOCKERs, all three MAJORs and all seven minors were reproduced against the tree at `02862c91d` (and against `lane/w-lot-a-1a@2fa724c1d`) before being applied. The register's own Rejected-false-positives section — the token-narrowing and bypass matrix, the ratchet's mechanical implementability and the `authz.self` allow-list boundary, W-LOT coexistence and the NULL-team ruling, the principals/writers/Sanctum/data-model chapter, and the static-guard, hardening, convention and wave chapters — is accepted as written and nothing in it is re-argued here.

**The register's "Preserve" list was honoured in full.** Direction B and settled D1–D8; **canonical post-rename adoption equality and the settled v0 definition `post-A-1a ∪ wave-0b additions`**, both untouched — B-1 is closed by giving `ensure` a *second* predicate, not by narrowing v0; exact add-and-remove template diffs for uncustomised roles with customised and custom-role protections intact — B-2 adds serialization around that diff and changes none of its semantics; W-LOT's marker semantics, NULL-team no-re-homing ruling and exact manager/general-manager grant sets; exactly two automatic sync triggers and no `DatabaseMigrated` listener — EC-37 constrains how the *existing* two interleave and adds no third; `admin = PermissionRegistry::activeKeys()`; the `326 / 177 / 149` census and the `167 / 148` **generated** ceilings, with only the post-0a read ceiling moving to **142** as M-2 directs; active `coupons.view`, the 22-key deprecation set and FE/POS-aware permission-shaped scanning; first-class service principals and `owner grants ∩ token scope` including the subject/target split and the unnarrowed POS PIN target payload; dedicated service-account role routes and typed refusal on the generic user-role routes; mutation-versus-gauge result semantics — `provisioned_during_run` is added as a **gauge** precisely to respect it; the human-only last-admin floor, `roles.view` read shaping and the audit-chain events; the two additive tenant migrations, central Sanctum token storage, the generated TypeScript union and en/fr/ar label coverage; the six-item wave 0a and its single accepted manifest overlap; and the real second-location test on `POST /stock-adjustments`, with the unattached `ValidateLocationAccess` middleware premise **not** restored.
